A missing MIRRORS row takes the whole ledger pipeline down — the canon check is a full-run gate

Details
Technical notes

A missing MIRRORS row takes the whole ledger pipeline down — the canon check is a full-run gate

Question

Graduated from 066. Twice now, a content gap in canon has

failed an entire watchlist-refresh run:

  • - 2026-08-06 — library/the-loop.md had no MIRRORS row (found by 062, fixed 136896ba).
  • - 2026-08-12 — library/job-35-messaging-triage.md had no MIRRORS row; four runs red across an hour.

The mechanism is structural, not accidental. Canon completeness check runs as a step inside

the same job as the ledger pollers, so exit 1 on a documentation bookkeeping problem marks

the run failed — and while the pollers themselves ran before it and their pushes landed, the

run's red status is what a human or a monitor reads. A missing MIRRORS row is a real defect

worth surfacing, but it is not a reason to report "the ledger tick failed".

Decide the shape:

  1. 1. Should the canon check gate the run at all, or report separately (its own workflow, its

own Run Error key, its own cadence)? The J28 pipeline's health and canon's projection

completeness are different questions with different owners.

  1. 2. If it stays in the run, should it be non-blocking (continue-on-error, file its own

distinct Run Error row) so a doc gap never masquerades as a poller failure?

  1. 3. Prevention: a new library/*.md with no MIRRORS row is a predictable authoring slip.

Should the mapping be enforced at write time (a pre-commit check, or the mirror row created

with the file) rather than caught hours later by a red tick?

Related, and worth settling in the same conversation: a re-run overwrites a run's visible

conclusion. Run 31858026100 shows success in gh run list while attempt 1 failed and

fired the Run Error handler. Anything that audits fleet health by reading conclusion — a

future invariant, a monitor, a sweep — will undercount failures unless it reads run_attempt

or the attempts endpoint. Name the rule so it lands in canon, not just in a ticket.

Acceptance

  • - A ruling on whether the canon check blocks the run, and where its failures get reported.
  • - A named prevention point for missing MIRRORS rows (write-time vs run-time).
  • - The re-run/conclusion measurement rule written into canon so audits stop undercounting.

📮

The list can tick off a booking by itself, but never a text message

Details
  • The whole point of the new list is that it doesn't care who did the job — it looks at the real booking and ticks it off
  • That works for bookings. It does not work for text messages: the proof lives in the message log, which the little sweep cannot reach from the cloud
  • Found the hard way — Junyan sent David Diep a text on Aug 7 at 9:42pm and the list still said 'waiting' eight days later
  • That night made the proof for us: the same job had two steps, move the booking and text him. The booking step ticked itself off the next morning. The text step never could
  • Nothing was broken. The sweep looked, couldn't see proof, and correctly refused to guess. Nothing else ever looked again
  • Three ways out, none obviously right: let the sweep reach the message log, or ring a bell when any step sits too long, or stop letting text steps claim a kind of proof nobody can check
  • Same shape as the missing thank-yous: the work was done, the notes were honest, and the finishing half was missing
Technical notes

A Next Action row whose proof is a human mark can never close itself

Question

The executor-agnostic close exists to remove the back-capture tax: VERIFY reads ground truth, so

it does not care who acted, and a row Junyan satisfied himself closes on the next tick with no

write-up. That works — for booking-class rows only.

Found 2026-08-15 while clearing the row that was blocking 053's

trigger. The row Reply to David Diep confirming the 509A switch sat at Ready from Aug 7 to

Aug 15. The message had gone out by hand on the night it was raised — threads/David-Diep-Skedda.md

carries the outbound SMS to +16474093506 at Fri Aug 7 9:42 PM, byte-identical to the row's

Continuation, with two replies from David the same evening. Eight days later the queue still

showed it as work outstanding.

The controlled experiment is already in the data. That incident produced two rows — the

booking move and the confirmation text — raised together, satisfied the same night. The booking row

(Proof type = booking, Booking ref 118753327 @ 2026-08-07 in 509A) closed itself on Aug 8,

Verified, exactly as designed. The message row did not, and could not. Same incident, same night,

same reviewer: the only variable is the proof type.

Nothing was broken. verify_row dispatches on proof type; this row is Proof type = human_mark

with no Booking ref, so it returns unverifiable and the tick leaves it exactly as it was —

which is the correct behaviour and deliberately so (045's

guard: a failed read must never read as evidence). The gap is that no other path ever revisits it.

So the question is not "fix verify" — it is: what closes a row whose proof lives somewhere the

tick cannot read? Three candidate answers, none obviously right:

  1. 1. Reach the ledger from Cloud Run. The proof for message-class rows is the J28 correspondence

ledger, and the tick has no checkout. This is the standing fog the map has carried since

043 (INV_LEDGER_URL, also why invariant I is

off). One venue decision would close both.

  1. 2. A staleness check aimed at the queue itself. Already ruled into

073 for mail rows — a row sitting past its Deadline with nobody

acting is a defect regardless of proof type. Cheapest, and catches classes nobody anticipated,

but it nags rather than closes.

  1. 3. Ban human_mark as a proof type for anything the ledger records. If an SMS's proof is

always an outbound in the ledger, human_mark on a message row is a mis-typed proof, not a

category of proof. Narrow, and makes (1) load-bearing.

Note the shape: the row was honest, the surface was honest, and the work was done. What was

missing is the same thing missing in 050 — the closing

half. There, messages died at Pending Review; here, the record of a sent message died at

Ready. Third instance of hold-and-forget on this map, and the first where the thing held was

already true.

Acceptance

  • - A message-class row whose send is in the J28 ledger closes without a human touching it,

observed once end to end.

  • - No row in the queue sits open past its Deadline without something, somewhere, saying so.
  • - Whatever is chosen, the unverifiable guard survives intact — a failed read must still never

close or flag a row.

#083
👻

Empty leftover door-code entries make the system think it has the wrong person

Details
  • When someone gets a door code, an empty placeholder entry is sometimes left behind under their name
  • The system then sees two entries with one name and stops, saying 'I cannot tell which of these is you'
  • That blocks real renters whose code is perfectly fine — it happened to Hanano (five leftovers) and Hannah (three)
  • The fix: ignore anything with no code on it. An empty entry opens no door, so it is not a second person
  • Two entries that BOTH open doors still stop the system — that one is a genuine mix-up and a human should look
  • Fix it in one place so every robot that uses door codes gets it at once
Technical notes

Make /provision ignore codeless shells instead of calling them ambiguous

Question

Ruled 2026-08-18 (in 081, where it was specified but not built —

it is an alarm-mcp change, not a change to any one caller).

/provision resolves a name through _verdict_of, which returns ambiguous whenever more

than one record matches — and a codeless shell IS such a match. Left alone, one stale shell

makes every later pass escalate a renter whose access is fine, and check_access_covers returns

manual_multiple, which blocks the HTA alarm-code gate for that person. Hanano I. (five

shells) and Hannah Cho (three) are the cases of record.

The fix is ignore, not reuse (Junyan ruled shell-reuse explicitly out — he clears the litter

by hand, and the Doorman's v6 (b5) duty now reports it): when counting matches for a name,

discard records with no effective code and panels_covered == 0. A codeless shell opens no

door, so it is not a second identity.

Two records that BOTH carry working codes stay ambiguous — that is the real conflict

(Sharon's 8206/4547) and must still reach a human.

Home it in _verdict_of inside /provision, so every caller — the booking sweep, the Doorman,

the same-day lane — gets it at once.

#089
🧵

One program will own a booking start to finish — Junyan's call

Details
  • Today two programs share the work: a fast one for bookings starting today or tomorrow, and the new sweep for everything further out. The handoff line between them is invisible from outside
  • Junyan ruled: one program owns the whole thing. It runs every half hour AND gets nudged the moment a booking email arrives, so it is both thorough and fast
  • The fast program retires after a week of both running side by side to prove nothing gets double-sent
  • The risky permission — texting a door code with no human check — moves with the work. We discussed the danger of putting that inside a bigger program, and Junyan accepted it: the real protection is the checks, not the small box
  • One new problem the build must solve first: with two ways to wake up, the program can wake up twice at once and do the same thing twice. It needs a simple lock
Technical notes

One job, two triggers — the sweep becomes the sole owner of the booking lifecycle

Ruling this implements (Junyan, 2026-08-19)

"I would prefer one agent handle the whole thing from start to finish." The split ownership

— SameDay.gs owns today/tomorrow, the sweep owns 2+ days out, handoff at an invisible

midnight line — is the discomfort. Chosen shape: one job, two triggers.

  • - The sweep chain owns the entire lifecycle: detection, artist record, alarm access,

board row, confirmation, and the same-day HTA — the deference branch dies.

  • - It fires on the 30-minute clock and immediately when a booking-confirmation email

arrives: the mailer keeps ZERO logic, it just POSTs the sweep's endpoint (?poke=true

or plain). Same code path on both triggers, so there is no seam to defend.

  • - SameDay.gs retires after a one-week parallel run, its dedup guards proven against the

sweep's (the Manuel Hernandez duplicate-HTA case is the regression to test).

  • - SameDay's auto-send authorization moves with the work, bounds unchanged: verified

panel access (pass/allow_permanent + panels_covered > 0) or nothing sends; canonical

HTR wording only; exactly-one-code-line check; placeholder check. The one-lane rule in

library/autonomy.md and library/fast-track.md must be REWRITTEN, not duplicated —

after this, "the same-day lane" IS the sweep's imminent branch.

What moves into the sweep

From SameDay.gs, in build order:

  1. 1. The imminent branch: booking starts today/tomorrow AND lacks its HTA → provision

(already built), pull the canonical fast-track HTA from the HTR DB, fill slots, verify

(one code line, no placeholders), queue at Ready to Send. The 45-min-late rule and the

staff/phone/identity refusals port as-is.

  1. 2. The mail poke: an Apps Script (or the existing send-engine project) forwards nothing

and parses nothing — it calls the endpoint on arrival of the Skedda confirmation mail.

Poke failure is harmless: the 30-min clock is the guarantee, the poke is the speed.

  1. 3. Platform mirror creation (Giggster/Peerspace/Tagvenue → Skedda booking with

read-back proof) — this makes ticket 084's "who owns them" answer THIS job; 084's open

questions (title-convention identity, missing phone at T−10) still need their grilling.

  1. 4. The T−2 confirmation gap closes the same way: the sweep's Pending Review confirmation

row currently carries no message body (caught 2026-08-19). With the HTR templating ported,

fill it from canon like everything else. No new wording is ever invented in code.

Order and safety

  • - Blocked on 082: the sweep must be live and boring on its

clock trigger before it takes over the riskiest window.

  • - Amended 2026-08-21 by 107: the parallel week is VOID.

SameDay.gs was never installed, so there is no running lane to shadow or compare against;

its dedup guards cannot be "proven in parallel" because they have never executed. Retirement

becomes a do-not-install note on the file, and 108

ships the record-only half of the takeover first. What 089 still owns is the SEND half: the

canonical HTA templating and the auto-send bounds moving into the sweep.

  • - ~~Parallel week: both lanes run, sweep in shadow for today/tomorrow (log what it WOULD do,

SameDay still acts). Flip = sweep acts, SameDay disabled — one Script Property, reversible.~~

  • - Junyan flips production. Update RUNTIMES.md, the Plumbing Map, autonomy.md, fast-track.md,

and the Doorman/Host prompts that name the same-day lane.

Acceptance

One owner: a booking made 30 days out and a booking made 40 minutes before start are handled

by the same code, differing only in which steps are due. SameDay.gs disabled with a

do-not-re-enable note. No renter receives two HTAs during the parallel week.

Implications discussed and accepted (Junyan, 2026-08-19)

Grilled before finalizing; Junyan accepted all three costs with eyes open:

  1. 1. Auto-send blast radius: the only unattended code-release grant moves from a small

sealed file into the desk's largest booking job. Accepted — the protection is the bounds

(verified access or nothing sends, canonical wording, one-code-line + placeholder checks),

which travel with the work. The middle position (one implementation, SameDay kept as a

thin second entry point) was offered and declined.

  1. 2. One job down = everything down: same-day access, future provisioning and confirmations

now share one failure domain. Accepted; the compensation is that a single job fails LOUD

(own 🚥 Run Monitor row) where the split failed silent at the seam.

  1. 3. Two triggers racing: the mail poke and the clock can fire concurrently; "check then

create" has a race window the single-trigger SameDay never had. The build MUST close it —

per-booking lock or Cloud Run max-instances=1 — before the poke ships.

#090
📥

The Day Sheet gets a bookings tab so Junyan can stop reading 18,000 emails

Details
  • Every booking today lands as an email in a Gmail folder — made, changed, cancelled. That folder has 18,404 messages and Junyan reads it by hand
  • Ruled: build the same view into the Day Sheet instead, straight from the calendar — one interface for everything
  • It must show all three kinds of event, not just new bookings. A cancellation you don't see is a renter you text about a booking they no longer have
  • So a cancellation inside the 2-day window also flags the board card and any queued message for cleanup
  • To spot changes and cancellations the program keeps a photo of the calendar between passes — a deliberate exception to its keep-no-notes design, and losing the photo loses nothing for good
  • Junyan stops reading the folder only after the tab has been right for a while, including through at least one real cancellation
Technical notes

The booking feed replaces the Gmail label — made, changed, and cancelled, on the Day Sheet

Ruling this implements (Junyan, 2026-08-19)

The Gmail label (18,404 mails of "AllBooked by Skedda") is Junyan's arrival view. Ruled:

the feed is a REPLACEMENT, not a supplement — "if I can replace reading gmail … I can

finally consolidate all of my communications into one interface." That raises the bar: the

label shows three event types — made, changed, cancelled — and the feed must cover all

three before Junyan can stop reading it. A creation-only feed would hide every cancellation.

Consequence accepted in the discussion: change/cancel detection requires comparing against

the previous pass's board, so the sweep keeps a snapshot between passes — a deliberate,

narrow exception to its no-stored-state design. The snapshot is a cache of reality, not a

processed-set: losing it re-lists a window of arrivals and misses nothing permanently

(changes/cancels against a lost snapshot re-derive on the next diff). Same posture as the

alarm watch's baseline.

  1. 1. Range read, one call. Refactor skedda_read.bookings_on (per-day) to a

bookings_range(start, end) — /bookingslists accepts an arbitrary window, so 45 days

costs one request, not 45. (The MCP's list_bookings_range already proves the shape.)

  1. 2. Horizon to 45 days (SWEEP_HORIZON_DAYS=45). Chain steps are already due-dated, so

the only behavioral change is that artist record + alarm access happen near arrival

instead of at day 14. NOTE the recurrence trap still holds: series records arrive

stamped with the SERIES start; the recurring skip stays (out of scope, map ruling

2026-08-19) and the range read must not change that.

  1. 3. The feed, three event types. Every pass diffs the current board read against the

previous pass's snapshot:

  • - Made — id present now, absent before (also caught by created > high-water mark).
  • - Changed — id present in both, but start/end/spaces/price/holder differ. Show

old → new, the way the label's "changed the booking" mail does.

  • - Cancelled — id present before, absent now, and the booking had not already ended.

THE CASE THAT MATTERS: a cancellation inside T−2 must also flag the already-created

FBS row and any queued confirmation for cleanup — a cancelled renter must not get a

confirmation text. Surface as a residue row, not just a feed line.

Render as a 📥 Bookings tab on the Day Sheet (deterministic renderer, J29 pattern):

holder, studio, start, price, event type, created-at, and what the sweep did about it.

  1. 4. Snapshot storage: the previous pass's normalized board, written where the sweep

already writes (a desk-state-style JSON in the repo, or GCS). Recomputable, small,

never consulted for whether to PROCESS a booking — processing stays derived from the

surfaces. Only the feed diff reads it.

  1. 5. Retiring the label is Junyan's call, later: after the feed has run visibly correct

for a stretch (including at least one real cancellation), he stops reading the label —

the mail keeps arriving as a backstop archive, nothing unsubscribes.

Acceptance

A booking made 30 days out appears on the feed within one pass of being made, with its code

already provisioned. The label becomes optional reading. RUNTIMES.md + Plumbing Map updated.

---

Rider added 2026-08-24 — the FBS board should ride this same read

Found while tracing platform bookings: the desk has two independent readers of the same

bookings. The 🛎️ FBS board is built from the Google Calendar feed — Source Event ID

holds calendar event ids (ptqd052iie4ctr9p5oi3ipska4), not Skedda's numeric booking ids

(119143355) — while the sweep reads the Skedda API directly. Skedda → Calendar → board is

three hops for the surface that actually drives HTA/EOB/PBF.

Consequences already visible:

  • - 118 of 602 board rows (~20%) have no Source Event ID, and it is not purely legacy —

Chelly Li's 26 Aug row was created 23 Aug with the key null while her 27 Aug row, created

24 Aug, has one. Rows are being written in two different shapes.

  • - A booking that fails to reach Google Calendar is invisible to the board, and the component

that would notice reads the other pipe. Same shape as the SameDay hand-off gap (107).

  • - Under 089's one-owner rule, a board that derives itself

independently of the sweep is a second writer.

Do NOT redesign the board row. Its event-keyed, holder-agnostic shape is exactly why

platform bookings work at all — keying it on the renter record would break that whole class

(see 084). The change is the feed, not the shape:

board rows come from the sweep's Skedda read, Google Calendar demoted to a display mirror.

Why here and not its own ticket: this ticket already builds the 45-day Skedda range read plus

the between-pass snapshot — precisely the read the board needs. Doing it first would build the

range read twice.

Progress 2026-08-24 — steps 1 and 2 landed

dc-canon commit 438d57ca, tested. Deployed 2026-08-25 14:39Z with 108 (bucket danceannex-sweep live, SWEEP_SNAPSHOT_BUCKET set) — first pass stores the board, diffs start on the second.

  • - skedda_read.bookings_range(first_day, last_day) — both ends inclusive, ONE request

however wide the span. bookings_on(day) is now a thin wrapper over it and keeps working

for presence and loop, which only ever ask about today.

  • - booking_sweep.read_board() is a single call instead of a per-day loop. A 14-day

horizon was 14 round trips; the span is now free, so the horizon becomes a policy choice

rather than a budget.

  • - Horizon default deliberately left at 14. The 45-day flip is SWEEP_HORIZON_DAYS=45

on the service — a config change Junyan makes, reversible in seconds, not a code deploy.

Provisioning access 45 days out rather than 14 is the real behavioural change and it

should be switched on deliberately.

  • - Four new tests on the read itself: one-request-however-wide, both-ends-inclusive

(an exclusive last day would silently drop the furthest day and nothing would say so),

single-day equivalence, and a backwards range refused rather than read. 393 pass

(test_app_relay/test_classifier/test_poller need fastapi, absent locally —

pre-existing, untouched by this change).

  • - The 13 sweep tests that monkeypatched bookings_on now patch bookings_range. This

mattered: left alone they would have stopped intercepting and attempted real HTTP —

a silent hole in the suite rather than a failure.

Step 3 is blocked on one decision: where the snapshot lives

Change and cancel detection needs the previous pass's board. The sweep runs every 30 minutes

on Cloud Run, so in-memory state will not survive — alarm_watch's in-process baseline

works only because it ticks every minute on a warm instance. event-gate today has no bucket

and no storage dependency (requirements.txt is fastapi/uvicorn/pytest; deploy.sh sets no

bucket env).

  • - GCS bucket — matches the watchdog-ledger precedent (gs://danceannex-alarm-watchdog).

Needs a bucket, an IAM binding, and a dependency. Recommended.

  • - Notion — no new infra, but a 45-day board is ~300 bookings and rich_text caps at 2000

characters per block, so it would need chunking. Ugly enough to reject.

  • - created high-water mark only — tiny state, fits anywhere, and this ticket already names

created as a second signal for made. But it cannot see changes or cancellations, and this

ticket's own ruling is that a creation-only feed does NOT let Junyan stop reading Gmail.

Proposed build shape that unblocks now: read the snapshot from SWEEP_SNAPSHOT_BUCKET if set,

and degrade cleanly when unset — made still works off created, changed/cancelled report

"no snapshot" rather than silently reporting nothing. Ships today, completes when the bucket

exists.

Re-scoped 2026-08-24 — the reading justification is gone, the mechanism matters more

Junyan, 2026-08-24: *"I don't have to read any emails anymore as it all goes through the desk

  1. 2. Change detection keeps a moved booking from leaving a stale board row and a confirmation

#091
🧩

Three more robots still look people up by name — finish the job

Details
  • We switched the two busiest robots over to ID numbers. Three others never got the memo and still go by name
  • The important one is the nightly code checker: it reads EVERY person's card and writes corrections into our records
  • If a card has no alarm name on it, that checker quietly falls back to the card's title — which never matches the panel. That is how you get hundreds of wrong 'this is broken' notes in one night
  • So: give it ID numbers first, and make it say 'I could not find this person' out loud instead of guessing from a title
  • While it is at it, it can fill in the missing ID numbers itself — it already reads every card once a day, so the gap closes on its own instead of needing another cleanup session
  • Also asked and answered here: can we delete the old alarm-name box? Not yet, and the reason is worth knowing
  • 529 people have a remembered door code but no entry on the panel any more. When one of them comes back, that old name box is what tells us how the panel spelled them
  • Delete it and they come back as 'Quynh Quinn QTrang Tran [Peerspace]' instead of 'Quynh Tran' — the exact mess we just cleaned up, all over again
  • So the last piece is deciding what to do about those 529 dormant cards. Once that is settled, the old name box can finally go
Technical notes

Finish the id migration — job D, morning prep and the attended skills still resolve by name

Question

085 moved the joins onto stable ids and built the one

trust ladder in alarm_monitor/identity.py — but it only migrated two consumers:

booking_sweep.py and the Doorman's job A. The rest of the fleet still asks the panel

for a name. Which of them move to the ladder, and what has to be true before

Alarm Display Name can actually be deleted?

What the audit found (2026-08-19, after the backfill)

Still name-keyed, not migrated:

| Consumer | What it does | Why it matters |

|---|---|---|

| alarm_monitor/code_mirror.py (job D) | daily, whole Artist table, WRITES Notion | keys on _plain(page, "Alarm Display Name") or _plain(page, "Name") — the or is the danger: with no display name it silently falls back to the decorated Notion title, which never matches the panel |

| deploy/morning_prep.py | FBS/Monitor pre-flight | same display or title pattern |

| process-studio-tickets (canon, attended) | staff ticket handling | canon still names Alarm Display Name the required lookup key (2026-07-27 rule) |

| send-engine/SameDay.gs | same-day access lane | 085 ruled this migrates opportunistically — confirm it is on the list, not forgotten |

The Artist DB as it stands (1,040 rows):

  • - 702 carry an Alarm Code (job D's scope) — only 167 carry an Alarm User ID. 24% coverage.
  • - Of the 535 coded rows with no id, 529 have no live panel user at all — the dormant

remembered codes. They can never acquire an id from a live lookup, because there is nothing

live to resolve. This tail does not drain on its own.

  • - Only 6 of the 535 have a live panel user; the display name resolved all six, the bare

title resolved none. Five of the six are already on 085's Review row (Manuel Hernandez ×2,

Jai Smith ×3); the sixth is Junyan's staff row.

  • - 67 rows carry a display name that DIFFERS from the title — the field holds information

nothing else does (Jose Luis Saldamando Salas [Authorized User] → panel Manuel Hernandez;

Quynh Quinn QTrang Tran [Peerspace] → Quynh Tran).

Why Alarm Display Name is NOT deletable yet (assessed 2026-08-19 — do not re-litigate)

Two independent reasons, either one sufficient:

  1. 1. The return path for 529 dormant renters. 079's ladder re-provisions a returning renter

with their remembered code. The display name is what carries the PANEL's spelling of their

name. Delete it and the only string left is the decorated title, so a returning renter is

recreated on the panel as Quynh Quinn QTrang Tran [Peerspace] — the original defect,

reintroduced through the back door, on 529 people.

  1. 2. Job D would not fail, it would lie. Dropping the property makes its or fall through to

titles table-wide and report differs/unknown on hundreds of rows — the several-hundred-

false-alarm disease this effort has already fought twice (the June alarm noise, the sweep's

four false residue rows).

Scope

  1. 1. Move job D / code_mirror.py to the ladder: resolve by the row's Alarm User ID first, fall

back to Alarm Display Name, and stamp the id back on a unique hit (fill-only) so its

daily whole-table pass becomes the thing that drains the coverage gap for anyone still live.

Kill the bare or title fallback — a row with neither an id nor a display name must be

reported as unresolvable, never silently matched on a decorated title.

  1. 2. Same for deploy/morning_prep.py.
  2. 3. Re-point the process-studio-tickets canon (and gates.md where it names the lookup key) at

the id, with the display name demoted to fallback — matching what

doorman-access-lifecycle.md now says.

  1. 4. Confirm SameDay.gs's position: migrate or explicitly defer with a reason.
  2. 5. Decide the dormant tail — the thing that actually unblocks deleting the field. Two

candidates, pick one: (a) retire the dormant rows outright (they are remembered codes for

people with no panel user and no bookings), or (b) have retirement/dormancy record the

last-known panel user id, so a returning renter is resolved by id and the name stops being

the only bridge back.

Retirement condition for Alarm Display Name (write it down, then hold to it)

The field goes when both are true: every consumer resolves through identity.py, and

the dormant tail is handled per step 5. Until then it stays, demoted — the second rung, never

the primary key. Neither is true as of 2026-08-19.

Acceptance

  • - No consumer reads Alarm Display Name as a primary key; every one of them tries an id first.
  • - No display or title fallback survives anywhere — unresolvable reports as unresolvable.
  • - Job D's daily pass write-backs are visible: Alarm User ID coverage climbs from 167 without a

second attended backfill.

  • - A dormant-tail decision is recorded (retire, or store the last-known id) — not deferred again.
  • - Canon updated in the same pass (process-studio-tickets, gates.md); RUNTIMES + Plumbing Map

entries; the retirement condition above lands somewhere durable.

Depends on: the alarm-mcp redeploy 085 is waiting on (Junyan's flip) — the code changes can

be written before it, but nothing is verifiable until the service carries identity.py.

Technical notes

JOB 3's mechanical reconcile moves to the event-gate tick

Question

Split out of 055 (lane 5) 2026-08-19, where it arrived via

Junyan's 2026-08-15 capacity ruling: walked through the four chronically-dropped jobs, he ruled

all four essential — *"they all seem really important; when they are missed they each cause

problems."* Right-sizing by deletion is dead; the fix is to take the mechanical work off the

LLM pass.

The principle: code finds the work, the LLM only does flagged work. Starvation exists because

scanning competes with judging inside one budget. Move every scan to the tick and the LLM pass

starts from a worklist — it cannot starve on finding, only on doing, and a growing flag queue is

visible in a way a skipped scan never was.

The sizing read (canon, 2026-08-15): JOB 3 is mostly mechanical. Message reconcile, strand

backstop, money reconcile and collection detection are all view-reads + status branches +

checkbox/Position writes. The HTR learning loop is already retired (the Drafted Body diff

field was deleted 2026-07-29). The one prose-shaped step — the orphan-gate audit's *"Current Block

names a staff task"* — has a structured equivalent the tick already uses on the new board:

Position = Gate:* with no open linked Action row and no Pending-Review queue row = orphan.

Rule: where the structured read is ambiguous, the code flags the row for an agent instead of

deciding — same contract as unverifiable. Never guesses.

Lands on the event-gate tick at a 10-minute cadence, beside /loop.

Acceptance

  • - The mechanical steps run on the tick; canon's JOB 3 names the tick as their owner.
  • - Ambiguity produces a flag for the agent pass, never a guess — pinned by test.
  • - Reporting per the standing rule: a zero pass reports zero; a Run Error row files if the

lane cannot run.

  • - 035's test: the new owner is seen acting before the

retirement counts as done.

#096
🗂️

Gmail files some mail away before anyone sees it, and nobody reads those piles

Details
  • The robot that sorts email looks in the inbox. But Gmail's own filters grab some mail first and file it straight into a folder — it never lands in the inbox at all
  • So the sorter never sees it. Not a bug in the sorter: it is looking where the mail is not
  • We have quietly known this twice and patched it twice, one folder at a time. Once in July for signed agreements, once in August for booking notices — that second folder had 18,000 messages nobody had ever read
  • Those two are the only folders the sorter checks. Every other filter that skips the inbox is still a blind spot
  • We measured it instead of guessing: 262 recent messages across seven folders, and not one of them ever passed through the inbox
  • The clearest cases are insurance (3 messages nobody has opened) and accounting (8). Unread proves it — the sorter marks things read when it handles them
  • Good news: the messages ARE in our records. This is not about losing them. It is that nothing acts on them
  • What needs deciding: for each folder, either name what should handle it, or write down 'this is noise and here is why'. Both answers close the hole. Having no answer is the problem
  • And a rule so the next filter someone adds cannot quietly make a new blind spot
  • One folder is its own question: the alarm folder holds 45,000 messages, and the alarm panels are now read directly. That mail may not matter at all any more
Technical notes

Auto-filed mail is invisible to the inbox sweep — the hole has been patched one label at a time

Question

Junyan, 2026-08-20, pointing at Artist Care - Insurance: *"There are actually multiple holes.

We have emails that get autofiltered by gmail."* He is right, and canon already says so twice —

reactively, one label at a time.

library/job-2-triage.md line 16 states the class outright: *"some notices auto-file straight to

a Gmail label and never carry INBOX, so the in:inbox scan is structurally blind to them."* The

response each time has been to bolt a per-label scan onto JOB 2:

  • - Dropbox Sign (Sales - Agreements) — added 2026-07-21
  • - Sales - Skedda — added 2026-08-13, after 18k threads had never once been read by the sweep

Those are the only two auto-filed sources JOB 2 scans. Every other filter that skips the inbox

is still a hole, and each was found by accident rather than by looking.

Measured, not assumed (2026-08-20)

262 messages sampled from the last 90 days, straight off the Gmail API:

| Label | Total | Unread | Sampled | Ever carried INBOX | Consumer |

Zero of 262 carried INBOX. The unread ones settle the ambiguity: JOB 2 marks what it handles as

read, so an unread message that never touched the inbox was never seen by anything. Insurance and

Accounting are the clearest — 3 and 8 messages nobody and nothing has ever read.

Note this is a processing hole, not a capture hole: gmail_pull.py queries after: with no

in:inbox, so all of this mail is in the ledger. Canon has it; nobody acts on it.

Decide

  1. 1. Per label, what should happen to this mail? Three plausible dispositions — a real downstream

(Insurance → a compliance artifact with an expiry date; Accounting → the Bookkeeper), the digest

class (071 — needs eyes, never action), or an explicit written drop

("this is noise, and here is why"). A label with no written disposition is the defect; an

explicit drop is a valid answer and closes the hole just as well as a consumer does.

  1. 2. The general rule, so the next filter does not mint the next hole. Something has to make a

new auto-filing label impossible to create without naming its consumer. Candidates: a canon rule

at filter-creation time, or a periodic reconciliation that lists labels with no consumer and no

drop reason and raises a row. Prefer the one that cannot be forgotten.

  1. 3. Artist Care - ADT deserves its own answer. 45,419 messages is the largest surface on the

desk by an order of magnitude, and the alarm watch now reads panels live

(055) — so decide whether the mail matters at all any more, or

whether it is now pure echo like the Skedda label (090).

Seam — read before building anything

The rules-router lane (068–074) will

eventually own labelling and routing. It does not decide the dispositions above; it implements

them. This ticket produces the per-label disposition table that 069

step 2's consumer audit needs anyway — do that work once, here, rather than twice. Nothing here

should wait on 068: the mail is unread now, and 068 is unbuilt.

Acceptance

  • - Every user label carries a written disposition — a named consumer, digest-class, or a drop reason.
  • - The general rule is decided and written into canon, not left as an intention.
  • - The ADT question answered on its own terms.

#102
🕵️

Watch whether Giggster hands the same phone number to a second guest

Details
  • The only open worry left from the throwaway-line investigation. Giggster's text numbers carry no clue about who is texting, and they come from a small pool
  • If a number gets passed to a new guest and we text the old one, that message goes to a stranger
  • We only had three weeks of texts to look at, which is nowhere near long enough to catch a reuse — so instead of guessing, we watch
  • Every Giggster text already contains the guest's name and a permanent conversation number. So: write those down next to the phone number each time, and shout the first day one number shows up with a second guest behind it
  • That single moment answers the question for real and tells us the interval. No test message to anybody
  • Same job also puts Giggster's own robot numbers on a do-not-save list, so they can never be filed as somebody's phone number
Technical notes

Prove whether Giggster recycles its SMS proxy numbers — passively

Question

Graduated from 100. Both email relays were cleared of

recycling risk on structural grounds (Peerspace embeds a mint epoch, Tagvenue a monotonic

enquiry id). Giggster's SMS proxy numbers carry no identity at all, come from a small

pool (226 / 249 / 289 / 365 area codes seen), and the observation window was only ~3 weeks

with 3 guest relays — far too thin to see a reuse cycle.

A recycled proxy reaches the wrong person. Settle it with evidence, not a probe.

Method

Log, on every inbound Giggster SMS: (from_number, conversation_uuid, guest_name, ts).

The UUID is in every body (giggster.com/messages/<uuid>?channel=sms) and the name in the

(Giggster) <Name>: prefix — both already present in canon, so this is a reader over

existing data plus a forward-looking append, not new plumbing.

Alarm the first time one number appears carrying a second UUID or a second name. That

event both proves recycling and dates the interval.

Also blocklist the platform-notification senders (+12898048305, +13656455150, and any

Giggster-sender body lacking the (Giggster) <Name>: prefix) so they never mint a contact

point — 100 found these already carry many guests'

business under one number.

No test send. Texting a possibly-recycled number is the exact mis-delivery being

investigated, and it would not measure the interval anyway.

Acceptance

  • - The detector runs over canon and continues forward; a first collision (or a stated

months-long clean run) is on the record with dates.

  • - The Giggster flip rule in 101 is either confirmed at

7 days or retuned to the measured interval.

  • - Platform-notification numbers are blocklisted from contact-point minting.

Seam with 101 (added 2026-08-20)

101 already writes the row this ticket wants to watch:

app.parse_giggster_relay reads (proxy number, conversation uuid, guest name) off every

guest-chat SMS and booking_sweep.record_relay_contact_point files it with no Artist

attached, deduped on (value, booking_ref). So the recycling alarm is a read over

☎️ Contact Points — one Value carrying two different Booking Refs is exactly the event

this ticket is looking for — rather than new logging. The blocklist half is still owed:

101 mints nothing for transactional notices (they lack the (Giggster) <Name>: prefix),

but that is a consequence of the shape check, not an explicit blocklist.

---

2026-08-25 — this ticket now blocks a concrete backfill, not just a theory

The Contact Points backfill of that day cleared all 10 Peerspace/Tagvenue guests who had

no contact point of any kind, and deliberately stopped at the 11 Giggster ones:

Dhan P., Sinéad B., Blessing, Dashanique J., Ghazal, CHEUK KI KEVIN L., Hailey D.,

abisha i., Shenay B., Sissy Maxwell "Isaura", Ramla.

Giggster hands over an SMS proxy number, never an address. Both email platforms are

recycle-proof by construction (Peerspace's mint epoch, Tagvenue's monotonic id), so an old

address is always safe to keep for recognition. A Giggster number carries no identity at

all, so a stale one filed as a contact point could attach a stranger to a customer's card

on the next inbound — worse than the gap it closes. That is this ticket's question, and

until it is answered the 11 stay unfilled.

So the acceptance test grows a second half: once recycling is characterised, either backfill

these 11 as recognition-only keyed on the conversation UUID (never the number alone, per

100), or record that they can never be filed and close the gap by ruling rather than by data.

relay recovery (this session).

Evidence threshold used (the ticket's first open question, now decided):

  • - Propose (Tier A) — the pair shares at least one hard signal: a stable contact-point

value (direct email or phone), a platform relay address, an Alarm user id, a Skedda user

id, or an alarm code, and at least one card is live. Hard signals are things a

stranger cannot coincidentally share.

  • - Propose as probable (Tier B) — no shared row in the databases, but conversation or

era evidence links them (cross-platform arcs; same name + relay minted in the same

enquiry window). Junyan's judgment is the only closer.

  • - Leave alone — name similarity with distinct hard signals (provably two people), or

closed×closed pairs (dormant; no sweep cost; listed in the appendix so they aren't

re-derived next quarter).

  • - A shared alarm code alone between different names is NOT identity — codes get reissued

(see appendix C).

Survivor suggestion = the card with the richer history / live product; it is a suggestion only.

#105
🗂️

Teach the send lanes to pick a door — stable-first, reply on the thread's own relay

Details
Technical notes

Teach the send lanes to pick a door — stable-first, reply on the thread's own relay

Question

101 made the desk able to find a person by any of

their contact points. It deliberately did not teach any lane to choose one. A person

now routinely has several doors, so every outbound message has a choice to make, and

nothing makes it.

The rule is already ruled (098, Junyan in session):

  • - Replying inside a platform thread → that thread's/booking's own relay row.
  • - Everything else — proactive outreach, invoices, door codes → stable rows

(real email / real phone) by default; a relay only as a last resort.

  • - "Latest relay wins" survives only as the tiebreak within a channel when there is no

thread context.

What has to be decided

  • - Where the choice lives: in the Message Queue row as it is written, or in the send engine

as it drains. One place, not both — 088 is

what two writers cost.

  • - How a queue row carries its thread context, so "reply on the thread's own relay" is

something the sender can actually evaluate rather than infer.

  • - What happens when the only door left is recognition-only — refuse and raise, or send

and flag. (The flip itself is 100's.)

  • - Whether the same-day lane, which owns its own sends, follows the same rule or is

explicitly exempt until 089 dissolves that seam.

#106
🗂️

Provisioning a renter by hand mints a second card — nothing says one already exists

Details
Technical notes

Provisioning a renter by hand mints a second card — nothing says one already exists

Question

alice hamman had two Artist cards on 2026-08-20. The cause, read directly rather than

assumed:

| card | created | by | carried |

|---|---|---|---|

| 3c275032…d208c | 03:02Z | Dance Annex Desk CLI (bot) | Company, Product |

| 3c275032…51b98 | 14:25Z | Junyan, by hand | alarm code + user id, Permanent Codes, Activity note, the FBS board row, the queued How-to-Access |

Same email, same phone, same Skedda User ID. No lane wrote twice. The desk created

her card at 3am; a human created a second one eleven hours later while setting up her door

code, because nothing in that path surfaces the card that already exists.

So: what stops the next one?

Why it matters more than one merged card

It cost a real thing. Until the merge, every 30-minute sweep hit her and returned

artist:ambiguous — the sweep correctly refuses to guess between two cards — so her

booking could not complete on any pass, for eleven hours. A hand-made duplicate is not a

cosmetic tidiness problem; it stops the deterministic lane dead for that renter.

It is also the class 098 could not see. That grill

measured the relay duplicate class (unbounded, platform-driven) and the private-vs-group

class (bounded). This is a third: human-driven, and invisible to every count we took,

because both cards look native and share every identifier.

What has to be decided

  • - Where the check belongs. Alarm provisioning is the moment the duplicate got made, but

the general shape is "a human is about to create an Artist row" — that may be one place

or several.

  • - What it shows. find_artist already answers this exactly (id → contact point → name),

and would have returned her existing card from the email alone. The question is what

surface puts that answer in front of a person mid-task without slowing the job — the

077 lesson applies: a check nobody reads is not a check.

  • - Whether the desk should instead detect the pair after the fact, on a cadence, and

raise it — cheaper to build, but it means the sweep stays blocked for that renter until

someone clears it, which is what happened here.

  • - Whether this changes the create_artist path at all. The bot behaved correctly

throughout; nothing about it needs fixing, and a fix aimed at the robot would miss.

Known count

Unknown, and worth measuring before designing: how many of the 25 duplicate-name sets

are this class (both cards native, shared stable identifiers) rather than the relay class?

104 will surface the evidence per pair — this ticket wants

the split.

Technical notes

The sweep takes the same-day window, record-only — delete the deference branch

Ruling this implements

107 — Instant bookings fall into the Registrar/same-day-lane deference gap:

the Registrar defers today/tomorrow bookings to send-engine/SameDay.gs, which has

never been installed. Every same-day booking since the sweep went live

(2026-08-20) has been deferred to nothing, silently and forever. Stage A closes the

hole without moving any send authority — that half stays with

089.

The change

In dc-canon/services/event-gate/booking_sweep.py:

  • - Delete the deference branch (~line 1101, result["deferred"] = "same-day lane

owns this booking") and in_sameday_window's gating role. Inside the window the

sweep runs the same steps it runs outside it: artist record, verified alarm access,

board row, confirmation queued at Pending Review.

  • - The one step that stays out is the same-day HTA. In its place, file an Actions

row + Ops Lead SMS naming the booking, the verified code, and what is owed:

"same-day booking, HTA not sent, human sends." Loud, attended, never silent.

  • - Keep the sweep stateless and idempotent — the row is re-derived per pass, so a

repeat pass must not file a second Actions row for the same booking.

Canon that ships with it (part of done, not follow-up)

  1. 1. booking_sweep.py lines 25–29 — the docstring asserting SameDay.gs owns the

window. Rewrite: the sweep owns it, record-only, HTA pending 089.

  1. 2. RUNTIMES.md lines 69–70 and the booking-sweep row — same false seam.
  2. 3. JOB 2's Skedda-label branch: "self-serve calendar noise — mark read only" applies

only when the holder is a known artist; unknown holder → last-minute path.

  1. 4. day-sheet/plumbing.html change-log entry (route + failure mode changed).

Approval

Production behaviour change on a live service — Junyan approves before deploy. Ask at

the same time: SameDay.gs stays uninstalled through 089 (do not switch it on as a

stopgap; it carries the unattended code-send grant).

Acceptance

Replay the Sara Aljabery case (booked 21:59Z for 13:15 next day, 509B): one pass

produces an artist record, a verified code, a board row, a queued confirmation, and

the confirmation + HTA queued for a human to send. deferred appears in no result.

Ruling 2026-08-25 (evening) — the window goes fully robotic. Implemented + DEPLOYED.

Junyan: *"bypass all human gates for any same day bookings (or bookings the next day) along

with any last minute changes that are just moves."* Found while tracing real short-notice

bookings: the same-day HTA was never composed at all (the Host writes the T-24 batch once

daily at 04:00; a mid-morning booking misses it and nothing re-runs it — Gabrielle Curate

2026-08-11 reached the door with a code nobody had sent her), and the first-timer

confirmation promised "a text 24 hours prior" that was already impossible.

Built in 12289d34, deployed 16:49Z (bc94f9f61430 verified, 434 tests):

  • - Same-day/next-day confirmation and HTA queue at Ready to Send — the send engine

dispatches them with no human tap. HTA wording is the canonical How-to-Respond card;

walkthrough link lifted from the last Sent HTA per studio; hard guards (exactly one

Alarm Code: line, no unfilled placeholder) stop the lane rather than send a fragment.

  • - Returning renters (any prior Sent HTA) get the confirmation only — no re-onboarding.
  • - Pure time-moves settle themselves (the David Diep shape): unsent rows voided

Will Not Send, board row re-dated in place, same pass re-queues at the new time.

Room-moves and moved-after-a-Sent-text stay human — a Review row says exactly why.

  • - SWEEP_SAMEDAY_HOLD=1 restores the human gate for this window without touching the rest.
  • - Why this is safe where SameDay.gs was not (Charlie/Angus, 2026-08-20): the sweep reads the

live board minutes before queueing — a cancelled or moved booking cannot reach the

send step on stale facts.

Honest remaining gap: a booking made <30 min before start can begin before the next pass

(Vladimir Sidorkin booked at 27 min). Closing that needs the mail-poke (089's territory).

INCIDENT + rollback 2026-08-25 evening — the window is HELD, not robotic

The fully-robotic build above (12289d34, 16:49Z) texted nine renters by 20:31Z on its first

afternoon. No wrong codes and no security exposure, but:

  • - three regulars received the same code twice in one day;
  • - four were sent first-timer onboarding they did not need;
  • - two sends walked past a human's explicit Will Not Send.

SWEEP_SAMEDAY_HOLD=1 went on at 20:47Z and is set on the service now — the pair is composed

exactly as before and waits at Pending Review. Nothing about this ticket's chain was reverted;

only the send authority.

Root cause of the returning-renter half: prior_htas() counted only rows this sweep had

tagged with its own Template field, so the Host's 141 access rows (6 tagged) were invisible

and nearly every real renter read as a first-timer. Fixed by matching access texts by SHAPE

(_HTA_SHAPED: template OR title Access/HTA OR body "Door Code"). This is the same disease as

canon 845c93f3 — a gate that reads only its own lane's bookkeeping and calls the rest of the

world empty. Worth generalising: any "have we done this before?" check that queries one lane's

own paperwork is wrong by construction.

Three gates, deployed in 6725b236 (live in a25606b8 / revision event-gate-00074-5dc),

one auto decision computed once in process(), each independently sufficient to hold, and holding

means Pending Review — never a skip:

  1. 1. booked_late — last-minute means booked late, not starting soon. Skedda's read carries

no created-at, so the definition is the snapshot's made-list: a booking the previous pass had

never seen. No snapshot = everything held.

  1. 2. human_said_no — any Will Not Send for that renter on that date holds the pair. The WNS rows

were on Caryl's and Anita's confirmations, but the human's decision was about the renter and the

day; the gate honours it at that width. Fails closed.

  1. 3. sameday_autosend — the master hold switch, unchanged.

539 tests pass.

Open decision: whether to clear SWEEP_SAMEDAY_HOLD. The gates are live, so the hold is now

belt-and-braces rather than the only guard — but taking it off is a send-authority change and is

Junyan's alone. Until then the same-day window behaves exactly as it did before 16:49Z.

Lesson for the record: the ruling ("bypass all human gates for same-day") was sound and is not

being second-guessed. What failed was the definition — "same-day" was implemented as starts

soon, which swept up every regular with a booking today, including ones a human had already

decided about. The gates encode the definition Junyan actually meant.

Junyan's position on the hold, 2026-08-25 evening

"Nothing is owed. I will send out the confirmation/hta until I am confident that what is being
queued up is right."

So the hold is held on purpose, not pending a fix. Do not propose clearing

SWEEP_SAMEDAY_HOLD as housekeeping, and do not treat it as an unfinished switch: what clears it

is evidence, and the evidence is Junyan reading composed pairs as they land and finding the right

renter, the right code, and no onboarding text sent to a regular. No customer follow-up is owed

for the nine sends — ruled, not deferred.

Note the shape of the remaining risk: the gates decide whether to send, and they are tested.

What is being watched now is a different question — whether the content the sweep composes is

right for the renter it names. That is exactly what the incident got wrong (right code, right

booking, wrong audience), so watching the wording rather than the plumbing is the correct check.

Put booking-site bookings on our calendar the moment they come in

Technical notes

Hold the room — a caller that mirrors platform bookings into Skedda on arrival

The defect

A Giggster/Peerspace/Tagvenue booking is not on the Skedda calendar until a human puts it

there. The mirroring logic exists only inside send-engine/SameDay.gs, which has never been

installed (see 107). So in practice Junyan mirrors

these by hand, and the room is held only when he is at the desk.

Proof — Julia S., Peerspace, Sun 23 Aug 19:00–21:00, 509A, $64. Confirmation mail landed

23 Aug 05:50Z (01:50 Toronto), 17 hours ahead. Skedda booking 119616803 was created

24 Aug 15:40Z — a day after the session ended. For the whole booking, 509A was free on our

calendar and could have been double-booked. Contrast Kim M.: mail 15 Aug 16:17Z, mirror created

16:24Z, seven minutes later — same path, working, because someone was awake.

This is upstream of everything else this map has built. 108

and 089 both read the Skedda board; a booking that was never

mirrored is invisible to both. Neither ticket helps Julia.

Corrected 2026-08-24. An earlier draft of this ticket implied platform bookings reach no
automation. They do: cards, verified alarm codes and FBS board rows all exist for ~20 Peerspace
guests, because the board is built from the Skedda calendar by booking title, not by holder.
Kim M. has code 6205 live on 4 panels and a board row for 27 Aug; Julia's HTA and EOB both read
Sent. That makes this ticket narrower and more clearly correct: holding the room is the
single broken step, and it is the whole job.

>

Junyan ruled 2026-08-24: platform bookings stay casual, with no Skedda user — never
create Skedda accounts for platform guests. create_user emails them a registration invite,
which settles it independently. So the holder stays null by design and the title is the
identity link — see 084 Q2, now load-bearing.

Scope — the room, and only the room

Hold the room automatically. Everything downstream (artist card, alarm code, board row,

messages) stays where it already is, and the identity questions stay with

084, which is still ungrilled.

Do: parse the platform confirmation mail, map listing → studio, call the mirror, verify.

Do not: provision access, create Artist cards, queue or send anything, attach a Skedda

holder, or create a Skedda user (it emails the guest an invite — ruled out 2026-08-24).

This deliberately does NOT install SameDay.gs. That file carries the unattended

send-a-code-to-a-stranger grant; this is a separate lane with no send authority, no alarm

access, and its own kill switch.

The trigger is the ACCEPTANCE (Junyan, 2026-08-25)

*"The issue with this one was because I accepted the booking through Peerspace. This

acceptance should have automatically set everything up immediately."*

This sharpens the Julia story and the design. The "Your booking is confirmed" mail exists

because the host accepted the request — Julia's request landed 04:26Z and Junyan accepted

at 05:50Z (1:50am Toronto, from his phone). So the human decision was already made, at night,

in one tap. What failed is that the desk gave that acceptance no effect: holding the room

required a SECOND manual step that merely duplicates the yes already given.

Consequences for the build:

  • - Mail-triggered IS acceptance-triggered. The confirmation email is the acceptance's

receipt, so a caller that fires on that mail fires on the acceptance itself. No new

Peerspace integration is needed — the event already reaches us.

  • - Latency target is minutes, not a half-hour clock. The acceptance is the go signal;

the lane should live where the 1-minute tick already runs (the send-engine Apps Script

project), not wait for the sweep's 30-minute pass.

  • - Acceptance cascades with no further human step. Mirror → the sweep's next pass finds

the booking → card, code (110: due on first sight), board row. One tap at 1:50am should

leave nothing owed by morning except the messages humans send by rule.

The build

Most of it exists and is proven.

  • - The route is live and idempotent. POST /mirror-booking on skedda-mcp

(revision skedda-mcp-00043-6j4 as of 2026-08-24 — the ticket previously named

skedda-mcp-00020-bxk, long superseded) creates the booking and then

re-reads the calendar, answering 200 only when it finds it; a repeat call on the same slot

returned created:false rather than double-booking. Verified live 2026-08-12.

  • - The parsers exist, in SameDay.gs: sdParseGiggster_, sdParsePeerspace_,

sdParseTagvenue_, plus sdStudioForListing_ which resolves listing → studio through the

🏢 Studio Reference DB (ds 0d774bae-1b5a-4c0f-9b22-068ba13ba18e) rather than keyword-guessing.

  • - The credential is settled (2026-08-24). The bearer is the SAME shared secret the

alarm side already uses: Secret Manager mcp-auth-token, project danceannex-skedda,

64 chars. Verified live against /mirror-booking — unauthenticated 401, authenticated

400 missing required field(s) (a deliberately empty body, so the probe wrote nothing).

There is no new secret to mint; the only open step is mounting it wherever the caller runs.

  • - confirm: true is not a human gate. Ruled by Junyan 2026-08-24 and recorded in

dc-canon/library/skedda-session.md: it is a machine interlock against accidental

writes. This lane sets it itself. Nothing here waits on a person to confirm a mirror.

  • - What is missing is a caller. Proposed shape: a new minimal lane in the existing Message

Queue Send Engine project — platformMirrorTick(), its own time trigger, its own kill switch

(PLATFORM_MIRROR_ENABLED), reusing the parsers verbatim and stopping at the mirror. Same

seen-marks discipline as SameDay (sdSeen_ / sdMarkSeen_) so one mail mirrors once.

  • - Failure escalates the way SameDay's does: an ✅ Actions row + Ops Lead SMS naming the platform,

the renter and what could not be mapped. An unmappable listing books nothing and says so —

guessing a room is worse than not holding one.

Two discrepancies to settle in the build

  1. 1. Title convention — RULED (Junyan, 2026-08-24): <Name> <Platform>. e.g. Kim M. Peerspace.

No brackets, no "FBS" suffix, no "Peerspace Booking," prefix, and NOT the

<name> (<Platform>) form sdMirrorSkeddaBooking_ currently writes — that line must be

changed before this ships. Since the holder is deliberately null, this string is the only

identity link a calendar booking has. Existing variants in the wild (Julia S. Peerspace FBS,

Peerspace Booking, Julia S.) should be normalised as they are touched, not in a bulk rewrite.

  1. 2. Price parsing. sdParsePeerspace_ reads Space rental; Julia's mail shows Payout

$64.00. Confirm which field the confirmation mail actually carries before trusting the number

— a wrong price on a mirrored booking poisons the money reads.

Acceptance

Replay Julia's real confirmation mail (thread 1a02d2b451c853b1, 23 Aug 05:50Z) against the lane

in dry-run: it resolves the "Sunny Dance & Yoga Studio with Ombré Curtains" listing to 509A,

Sun 23 Aug 19:00–21:00, and would create exactly that booking — then a second pass creates

nothing. Live: a platform confirmation arriving overnight has its room held before morning.

Built 2026-08-24 — send-engine/PlatformMirror.gs

The lane is written, standalone (no dependency on SameDay.gs, which can now go),

and covered by test_platform_mirror.js — 28 tests, all passing, run with

The parsers are pinned to REAL mail bodies, and that caught three live bugs

that would each have meant a silently unheld room — the old parsers were written

against the Gmail API's pipe rendering, not GmailApp.getPlainBody():

  1. 1. Julia's mail has no "Space" label; the listing is the line after "Booking details".
  2. 2. Peerspace separates date and time with a literal <br/>, not a newline.
  3. 3. Peerspace indents its labels, so a ^Payout anchor never matched.

Both discrepancies in the section above are now settled:

  1. 1. Title — <Name> <Platform>, implemented in pmTitle_. Confirmed as what

Junyan already does: the live bookings read Chris Giggster and Kim M. Peerspace.

  1. 2. Price — the PAYOUT, not the gross, settled against four hand-made

mirrors rather than by preference: Julia 64 (payout 64), Kim 117.60 (payout

117.60, gross 147.00), Chris 145.80 (payout 145.80, headline 180), Jessica T.

48 / Angelica B. 80. Both parsers changed accordingly. Tagvenue remains

UNVERIFIED — no hand-made Tagvenue mirror existed to check — and is flagged

Preflight PASSED 2026-08-25 17:00 EDT — the lane is fully proven bar a real booking

platformMirrorPreflight run from the Send Engine project:

``

SKEDDA_HTTP_URL : https://skedda-mcp-249118612005.us-central1.run.app

SKEDDA_HTTP_TOKEN : set, 64 chars

PLATFORM_MIRROR_ENABLED : true

OPS_LEAD_PHONE : +16477733887

Both routes answered from Apps Script's own network with the project's own token, so the

The trigger check was added to the preflight this session — properties can be perfect and

the lane still dead if nothing calls the tick, and that failure is SILENT (it looks exactly

like "no platform bookings came in"), which is the Julia failure mode all over again.

#111
🗂️

A mirrored room is held once and never given back — cancellations and changes

Details
Technical notes

A mirrored room is held once and never given back — cancellations and changes

Why this is not ticket 084

Junyan asked for this as "084, it's a huge gap." 084 is closed and correctly so — its

four questions (who owns platform bookings, title-convention identity, the missing phone,

repeat-renter short-circuit) were all answered on 2026-08-24. This is a different gap that

was mis-pointed at 084 in conversation: 084 is about identity and ownership, this is

about the lifecycle of the mirror itself.

109 built a lane that HOLDS a room when a booking site

confirms. It had no way to give one back.

The defect

A booking site's confirmation is not final. Renters cancel, move and extend. The mirror

lane wrote rooms in one direction only, so the calendar drifts away from the platform and

never drifts back.

A phantom hold is worse than an unheld room. An unheld room can still be sold — the

failure is a missed booking. A room held for a booking that no longer exists blocks a

real renter silently, for as long as nobody looks, and there is no signal anywhere

that it is wrong.

Proof — Leah, Giggster, 509A, Mon 24 Aug 11:00–12:00.

| When (UTC) | What happened |

|---|---|

| 18 Aug 00:56 | "Instant Booking Details" — approved and paid, $32.40 payout |

| 18 Aug 01:07 | "Your booking has been canceled" — Reason: Cancelled by Renter. Eleven minutes later. |

The calendar is empty for that slot, which is correct. But the lane as built on 24 Aug

would have held 509A at 00:56 and never released it — blocking that hour for six days

until the session passed. Found while checking a booking I had wrongly reported as an

unheld room; the empty slot was right and my reading of it was wrong.

What shipped, 2026-08-24

Cancellations are handled end to end.

  • - New route POST /release-booking on skedda-mcp (skedda-cli/hosting/server_http.py).

Deletes the mirrored booking and verifies by absence — a separate read that can no

longer find it. Idempotent: releasing an already-absent booking is a 200, so a re-delivered

cancellation mail is harmless.

  • - Three guards, all server-side so a buggy or compromised caller cannot talk the server

into deleting a real booking. Extracted to release_refusal() and tested directly

(hosting/test_release_booking.py, 8 tests) because this is the only destructive path

on the server:

  1. 1. Holder must be null. Every platform mirror is casual with no Skedda user (ruled

2026-08-24). A holder proves the match is wrong. This is the guard that makes a wrong

match survivable — the worst outcome becomes a refusal.

  1. 2. Title must match exactly what the caller expected. For a holder-less booking the

title is the only identity link there is.

  1. 3. Never a recurring series — cancel_booking on a series id deletes every occurrence,

past and future. No platform mirror is ever recurring.

  • - The lane (send-engine/PlatformMirror.gs) now remembers what it holds

(PLATFORM_MIRROR_HELD) so a cancellation naming only a renter can find the room. It

releases only when exactly one room is remembered for that renter; zero or more than one

escalates rather than guessing. Rooms whose booking has already started are pruned.

  • - Cancellation detection is gated on the subject only. The body is unsafe: a guest asking

"what is the cancellation policy?" arrives as an ordinary message mail, and Giggster's own

booking mails cite the "cancellation and refund policy" in their footer. Both are pinned

as tests.

Tests: node send-engine/test_platform_mirror.js — 36 passing, including the full

hold-then-cancel cycle replayed on Leah's real mail bodies.

Still open

  1. 1. Booking CHANGES are not handled. Giggster "Booking update has been approved" (a renter

moving times, adding hours, changing attendees) is ignored, so the room stays held at the

ORIGINAL time. Real and observed: Hanano's 22 Aug booking was modified from 13:00–16:00 to

12:30–16:00. Needs an /update-booking route with the same three guards plus a read-back

on the NEW time, and a caller that recognises the update mails. Note the update mail may

not carry enough to identify the original booking — the lane's own memory is likely the

join, same as for cancellations.

  1. 2. Peerspace and Tagvenue cancellation shapes are unverified. Only Giggster's real

cancellation mail was available on 2026-08-24. The subject gate is deliberately broad

(/cancell?ed|cancellation/i) to catch theirs, but the renter-name extraction is pinned

only against Giggster. Check on the first real one.

  1. 3. /release-booking is written but NOT DEPLOYED. Until ./deploy.sh runs in

skedda-cli, the lane's release call 404s — which escalates with an Actions row and an

Ops Lead text naming the room to free by hand. That degradation is tested, so the lane is

safe to enable before the deploy; it just needs a human for cancellations until then.

Approval

/release-booking deletes calendar bookings. It wants one supervised release before it runs

unattended. Deploying it is ./deploy.sh in skedda-cli — which rebuilds the image and

updates both the skedda-mcp service and the skedda-refresh job (never a partial

deploy; canon library/skedda-session.md).

#039
🕵️

Always know WHO sent every customer message

needs 038 first
Details
  • On Jul 30, eleven held messages went out in one hour — and no record said who did it
  • It was Ela doing her job, but a runaway program would have looked exactly the same
  • The sending robot stamps its own name over the person who pressed go
  • Fix: the robot notes who handed it each message BEFORE stamping, and AI helpers announce a send before making it
  • Then a checker matches every real sent message to a known sender — anything unmatched raises a flag
Technical notes

Send attribution — every outbound must match one authorised origin

Question

Raised on the 2026-07-31 fixer run, from two live incidents:

  1. 1. The Jul 30 bulk release — 11 held 📤 Message Queue rows (several carrying

explicit DO-NOT-RELEASE prose) flipped to Sent in one hour. It took a day and a

direct question to Junyan to learn it was Ela working the queue, because Notion

keeps only last_edited_by and the send engine overwrites the releaser's identity

seconds after every release (created_by is also useless — every agent writes

through Junyan's integration token). A staff member clearing her list and a

runaway program are indistinguishable in the record.

  1. 2. The direct-send hole — an LLM running process-studio-tickets (or any skill with

send_sms) can put a message on the wire with no queue row at all. The J28 ledger

records it as source: api-sms-poll — capture method, not actor. Phone.com's raw

API confirms (checked 2026-07-31): no user/device/client attribution exists at the

carrier; everything sends as ext 100 "Dance Annex".

The design (ruled directions, 2026-07-31 fixer session)

Reconcile, don't enumerate. The invariant: *every outbound message in the J28

ledger matches exactly one authorised origin.* Three parts:

  • - Release capture (send-engine hook, NOT a poller). Junyan rejected the 3-min

poller as too much machinery. Instead the Apps Script send engine (repair 029)

reads the row's last_edited_by BEFORE it claims/overwrites the row — at that

moment it is still the releaser — and writes the actor into its send record.

Event-driven, zero polling, cannot miss a send.

  • - Send-intent declarations (direct-send lane). Any skill/agent authorised to send

off-queue writes a one-line intent to the ledger (who, recipient, body hash, ticket)

BEFORE calling send_sms/Gmail. An undeclared send must never look authorised.

  • - The reconciler. Rides 038's queue-row ↔ ledger-outbound join (build the join

once — this is why this ticket is blocked-by 038), extended with the intent lane:

every ledger outbound matches a released queue row or a declared intent. Unmatched

outbound → Attributed defect (Causing Job = send path), surfaced on the Fixer

Report / night watch like 038's mismatches. Derived checks, all from the same data:

unknown actor, Pending-Review→Sent gate bypass, per-actor release bursts,

hold-language-in-Reasoning released while its gate was unsatisfied.

Out of scope, related

The deeper cure for the hold-violation class — a real Hold Until/Release Gate

property on the queue so held rows never appear in the staff working set (today holds

are prose in Reasoning, invisible at click time) — is a production schema change

Junyan has not yet ruled on. Raise it at ruling time; do not build it here.

Resolution records: the send-engine hook line (Junyan pastes it — production Apps

Script), the intent-declaration convention + which skills write it, the reconciler

(where it runs, match rules), and the first unmatched-send-or-clean-week readout.

That ordering is well-chosen: these four are the cheapest checks to get right, so they're also the

Ruled in 044: this roster stays separate from the Sentinel's. The Sentinel watches runs, these
