Technical notes

Build the reconciliation invariants (P, Q, R) — permanent human seat

Question

Graduated from 044. Re-blocked 2026-08-05 (Junyan): existence

ships first, so this waits on 047 as well as the lane — the

judgment-heavy family goes last, after the probation lane has been proven on the cheap checks.

These three checks are the ones 045's

cried-wolf lesson points straight at: a diff between two systems is usually a timing artifact, not a

defect. So they carry a permanent human seat — they never graduate to autonomous escalation.

Findings land in front of the Custodian's 09:35 reviewer every day, and only a human-marked-real

finding becomes an ✅ Actions row.

Deterministic diffs, owner on human-marked-real is the Ops Lead throughout:

  • - P — every good-to-go FBS booking has its due HTA/EOB/AVA queued
  • - Q — FBS board ↔ calendars: no duplicate rows, no orphan rows
  • - R — yesterday's logged hours ↔ Staff Scheduling (the Timekeeper's line)

Design question this ticket still owns: how much timing tolerance each diff carries before it

reports at all. 045's invariant G was noise precisely because it ignored Send After — the

scheduled-send feature — and read deliberate future-dated rows as stuck. Each of P/Q/R has an

equivalent: the legitimate reason two lists disagree right now. Name it per check before building,

or the review seat drowns.

No fire-repair authority (Ruling 2) — detect and escalate; the Ops Lead investigates.

#049
💸

Build the checks for bookings that never got billed

needs 046 first needs 047 first
Details
  • Two checks, waiting until the paperwork checks have proven themselves
  • First: did every unpaid booking get a bill raised — including yesterday's, checked again
  • That is the gap that let two bookings go unbilled on Jul 25
  • Second: a booking marked good-to-go where nobody seems to have shown up
  • The robot never works out a price. It only checks that a bill exists
  • A third one to add: a finished booking whose thank-you message never got settled within a day
  • House rule from Junyan: past one day, a thank-you is never sent. No exceptions, no judgement call
  • So the check has one day to notice, and after that the only right answer is mark it will-not-send
  • It must never nudge anyone to send a late one — that is the one thing the rule forbids
  • Settled means sent OR marked will-not-send. The robot only cares that somebody decided
Technical notes

Build the completeness invariants (S, T) — unbilled bookings and no-shows

Question

Graduated from 044; split out of the old combined

existence+completeness ticket on 2026-08-05 when Junyan ruled existence ships first, alone.

Blocked on 047 clearing, so the probation lane has a proven cohort

behind it before money-adjacent checks enter.

Date math and plan rates, escalating to ✅ Actions For = Ops Lead:

  • - S — every [Unpaid] booking has a Money Request Queue row, including the prior-day

back-check. This is the gap that cost two unbilled bookings on Jul 25 — the failure 035's

rehoming pass was supposed to close.

  • - T — a GTG booking with no arrival signal is surfaced

JOB 32 never derives a price (Junyan, 2026-07-23) binds S absolutely: the check asserts that a

money request exists, and never computes, suggests, or validates an amount. No fire-repair

authority (Ruling 2) — detect and escalate only.

Read guard for S (HOUSE-RULES): the Money Request Queue and ✅ Actions to Perform share one data

source (collection://20df225d-382f-4bb8-9c15-c31571c9f4e0). Any money read by collection id must

filter Type ∈ {Charge, Refund, Credit, Platform charge request, Invoice}, or Access/PIN, Decision,

Review and Run-Error rows come back as if they were charges.

U — a completed booking whose PBF never resolved. Added 2026-08-05 from

046's live read: seven bookings sat Complete + PBF = Unsent

for up to five days, unseen.

RULED by Junyan, 2026-08-05: a PBF more than 1 day past the booking is never sent. This is a

standing policy, not a per-case judgment — it makes U the sharpest check in the roster:

  • - The window is 1 day, hard. Not a tuning parameter, not something to pick after

050 reports. A booking Complete with PBF = Unsent

more than 24h after its booking date is a defect, full stop.

  • - The check is on resolution, not delivery. Sent and Will Not Send both clear it. U never

asserts that a message went out — only that somebody decided.

  • - Past the window there is exactly one correct resolution: Will Not Send. A late

"how did it go" reads worse than silence, and RESPONSE-RESOLVER step 2 forbids the tense mismatch

outright. So U's escalation text says mark it Will Not Send — it must never read as

"send the missing PBF", which would push staff into the one action the rule forbids.

Note what this rule does to the defect's shape: the damage is done at 24h and cannot be repaired

after. That makes U a deadline check, closer in kind to 042's ladder than to the rest of this

family — worth remembering if U ever starts to look like it wants escalation rungs.

Do NOT let U's escalation imply a send. The one way this check can do harm is by prompting a

stale PBF back out the door.

U is the only detector pointed at this event — confirmed by 050,

2026-08-05. Both existing tripwires are structurally incapable of firing on it: the Responder's

pass-ledger counters count skipped (step 7 ran every pass, so nothing ever climbed), and JOB 1

Step 5's "flag it if still Unsent the following day" has no query behind it — Step 1 pulls

today's rows only, and the 07-21 completeness sweep filters to Booking Status ∈ {Upcoming, In

Studio}, so a yesterday Complete + PBF = Unsent row is returned by neither. When U ships,

delete JOB 1 Step 5's prose flag or give it that query — an unfireable tripwire left in canon

beside a working check is worse than no rule, because it reads as coverage.

All three ship Provisional and graduate through 046's lane —

promotion gate, 5 consecutive clean review days.

#056
🤝

Merge the message-writing robot into the new loop

needs 053 first
Details
  • Junyan agreed: its job is narrow enough that it doesn't need to be its own robot
  • Why it matters: a robot that only sees one message can't know what step of a process we're on
  • That's what went wrong with Dana's booking — the invoice and payment deadline lines were missing
  • Junyan fixed it by writing it himself, and the system learned nothing — the new loop catches that
  • Not doing it yet: the loop has to prove itself first, and another plan uses this robot as its test subject
Technical notes

Fold The Responder into the advancement loop

Question

The end-state is already ruled (018 §5, Junyan: *"because the

responder's scope is so narrow I'm okay folding it in — worst case scenario I would just draft the

responses"*). This ticket executes it, and owns the coordination the ruling deliberately deferred.

Why it folds. The Responder was carved out 2026-07-29 to get one measurable job with one

accountable owner: single scope, signed work, held-by-default, promotion ladder, scorecard. The

loop has all four, generalised — a courtesy reply is just a ticket whose loop is one turn long.

The Responder is the loop, hand-built early, for one action class. Keeping both means maintaining a

"who composes what" treaty forever, and every composer boundary is a place where steps go missing.

The evidence it produces defects. Dana Hufe (Tagvenue, 509A Mondays Sep 28–Nov 16): Junyan

hand-corrected the sequence because the composed message was missing the invoice line, the

pay-within-24h confirmation condition, and the terms link. A composer that only sees "reply to this

inbound" cannot know where a procedure is up to. Worse, because he sent it himself off-queue, the

correction taught the system nothing — precisely what the loop's rewrite beat exists to capture.

What this ticket must resolve:

  • - Coordination with the self-improving-desk sibling — the

Responder is that map's probe; its scorecard and nightly critic hang off it. Folding is a shared

decision, not a unilateral one. Read the sibling's state first; never double-extract.

  • - Latency parity — the Responder's ~30-min "a draft exists before a human reads the inbound" is

a real property. The loop must match it for this class before absorbing it.

  • - Class mapping — courtesy / acknowledgement, quick answers, holding replies on nudges,

PBF-adjacent replies, PBF initial sends, the no-show text (with its Ready to Send carve-out and

the absolute 1-day PBF expiry rule) become action classes in the queue, most of them

fast-promoted since they are already trusted.

  • - Do not retire before the successor acts (035): the Responder

keeps running until its classes demonstrably run in the loop.

Until this lands, the pilot boundary holds: process-driven messages (steps of an open ticket

— offers, confirmations, invoices, payment chases) belong to the loop, which holds the procedure

state; stateless replies stay with the Responder. "Stateless" means no process state, not

deterministic — the truly deterministic quick answers already left to the event-gate card lane

(033).

Technical notes

The agent↔staff seam — try shapes live, then pick one

Question

Graduated from 018. JOB 3 (reconcile) and JOB 4 (handback) are both,

in Junyan's words, "related to figuring out what staff has done" — passive inference, hours after

the fact, which is where the F1/F2/F5 gate-discipline defects concentrate. He wants *"a much more

interactive process that can happen at a certain time in the day."*

Junyan ruled this cannot be grilled to an answer: *"this needs to be tested out as this seam

between agent and staff is very messy. We need to actually try out different things to see what can

work."* So this is a prototype series, not a design ticket — run cheap shapes against real days

and let the winner emerge. Do not write a spec first.

Shapes worth trying (not exhaustive — add what the days suggest):

  • - a scheduled desk stand-up at a fixed time: agent presents the open proposals and gates, staff

clear them live in one sitting, together

  • - staff-initiated — staff say what they did as they do it, agent reconciles immediately
  • - pure executor-agnostic drift — no session at all; the loop's verify reads close everything

observable and the seam simply shrinks to nothing (052 §3

may make the session unnecessary — test this before building a session)

  • - a daily digest the Ops Lead answers once

Judge on: gates cleared per day, how long a cleared-but-unmarked gate survives, and how much the

staff actually engage. The #desk-drops capture path is the fallback throughout, and Junyan wants

to need it less over time.

Blocked on 052 because the loop's queue is the surface any

session would work over — and because the executor-agnostic path may dissolve most of the problem.

Residue overflow backstop — the alarm that works when tokens are gone

A daily page listing the mail that needs eyes but no action

Technical notes

Mail Digest page — deterministic daily audit trail for digest-class mail

Question

Digest-class mail (FYI: receipts, stats, notices needing eyes but no action) gets a

deterministic daily page in the 019 renderer pattern (decisions, decision 9):

  • - One page per day: sender · subject · one machine-extracted line per item.
  • - Plus per-rule hit counts and a freshness stamp (the page doubles as the rules

stage's own health surface).

  • - Strictly an audit trail — state on the page that nothing here requires action;

every action routes elsewhere by construction. This is what distinguishes it from

the surfaces that failed before (those carried action-required signals).

  • - LLM garnish (a short summary block) optional, only when tokens exist; page must be

complete without it.

Decide here: own GitHub Pages repo vs a tab — but NOT a tab on the day-sheet action

surfaces (awareness and action stay separate).

Acceptance

two consecutive days; complete on a day with zero LLM passes.

#072
🎟️

Let the rules sorter start tickets for robot-written mail

needs 068 first needs 053 first needs 054 first
Details
  • Booking-system emails always look the same, so a program can start the ticket in minutes
  • The smart robot later adds judgment — but the ticket already exists, even with no tokens
  • Both ticket-makers share one duplicate-check, so no email ever becomes two tickets
  • Only proven mail shapes qualify; anything odd goes to the smart robot instead

Technical notes

Duplicate-send check — catch the same message going to one person twice

Question

Build the check that catches a duplicate send — the same message dispatched to the same

person twice in the same moment. Graduated from 015, whose

scan (evidence) found the class live and unwatched:

three renters received four copies each of a 2,261-character message inside one hour on

2026-06-21, plus doubled sends on 2026-05-28 and 2026-05-30.

Ruled by Junyan 2026-08-19, choosing this over two alternatives that were on the table:

a ledger-integrity check (rejected — the scan shows the store is sound: 0 cross-source

groups in 9,465 records, idempotency-by-msg_id verified on the wire) and folding all

duplicate work into 061 (rejected — 061 watches the

Notion projection, and no queue-side or projection-side check can see this class: the

📤 Message Queue holds one row per send, so a doubled dispatch reads as correct from there).

The key — tight, or it is noise

counterparty + direction + normalized body + identical timestamp to the second, outbound

first. The unbounded body-match the original ticket specified produces 511 hits, almost

all legitimate recurring sends — invariant G's disease (045)

repeated. Measured false-positive behaviour by window is in the asset; the same-second key

yields 23 groups across the entire ledger, of which the outbound customer-facing set is 8.

  • - Exclusions: the non-correspondence senders already configured in

config/ledger_exclude.json (Toronto Hydro, Paperform et al. account for 11 of 12 inbound

hits — genuine vendor re-sends, correctly recorded).

  • - Do NOT exclude +16477733887. The 015 scan set its test traffic aside by inspection and

flagged "should this be a rule?" — ruled no, 2026-08-19. Junyan: *"it's my personal

number I use to test stuff"* — and that is exactly why it stays in. The number does double

duty: it is the test line and the estate's on-call destination, roughly 7 alert records

for every test one. 274 records (198 outbound, 2026-05-13 → 08-18) carrying

🚨 alarm pages (509B, 693, 527 sounding), panel low-battery-with-power-failure, Sentinel

missing/retracted alerts, and fast-track notify-afters. Only 36 of 274 are test traffic

(13%) — excluding the number to suppress those would blind the check to the other 87%, the

most safety-critical stream on the desk. A duplicate-send storm to the on-call phone is

among the most valuable things this check could catch (alert fatigue burying a real page, or

a runaway loop paging repeatedly). Test traffic is identifiable by content, not by number,

and probably needs no filter at all: a doubled test send is noise a reviewer clears in one

mark on the probation lane, which is what the lane is for.

  • - Widening the window past one second is a deliberate later call, made on live data, not

at build time — a minute-wide window admits the recurring-template families. One live example

of what a wider window would catch, found in the 015 scan: 2026-08-15 15:58 and 15:59, two

near-identical "Hi Himanshi! …Studio 527" messages a minute apart, one carrying a door code and

one not — a preview or redraft rather than a fault, but exactly the shape the second-wide key

misses. Evidence for widening later; not a reason to widen now.

Coverage is a build requirement, not a nicety

The check must report the record count and time span it actually read, and fail loudly

rather than pass when it cannot read the whole window. This is not defensive habit: the 015

scan produced two clean-looking results over partial stores before the real one — a sparse

checkout hiding 96% of the ledger, then a parser that breaked on git cat-file's missing

lines and reported "0 unparsed" over 91% of it. A scan that does not assert coverage is

indistinguishable from a scan that found nothing, which is this map's recurring pathology

(the dead invariants checker serving a five-day-old snapshot; the PBF sweep truthfully

reporting found · deduped forever).

Lane

Ships Provisional on 046's probation lane — writes to

the Provisional block, files no Actions row, feeds no escalation counter, reviewed at the

Custodian's 09:35 pass. Per 044's standing rule it is tested against a day of live data before

it may escalate to a human, and it never counts its own output as desk state.

Venue follows the other invariants — event-gate on Cloud Run. Note the open constraint: the

J28 ledger is unreachable from Cloud Run today (the "ledger is unreachable" fog on the map;

INV_LEDGER_URL is the seam already built for it). This check reads the ledger, so it either

resolves that seam or states its dependency explicitly.

Acceptance

  • - Runs against the full ledger window and prints its coverage (records read, span).
  • - Reproduces the 2026-06-21, 05-28 and 05-30 groups from history.
  • - A day of live data with zero noise marks before it may escalate.
  • - Findings carry enough detail to act: counterparty, timestamp, copy count, message ids.

✅#087🐌The Registrar is too slow, and it ran out of time on its very first night2026-08-25
  • It was switched on at 10pm and its first unattended run was cut off after exactly five minutes, having not finished
  • Two separate time limits were both set to five minutes, so there was no slack anywhere — the first limit it hit was also the last
  • Both raised to fifteen minutes, which fixes tonight. It is not the real fix
  • The real reason it is slow: it asks the booking calendar for one day at a time, so a two-week look-ahead is fifteen separate requests before it even starts work
  • Asking for the whole fortnight in one request would do nearly all of it. The calendar already supports that
  • This matters because the run happens every half hour. If a run ever took longer than that, two would overlap — and two copies working at once is exactly what caused the duplicate door codes back in August
  • Deliberately NOT fixed by looking at fewer days ahead. That would just mean bookings a week or two out get set up late
  • DONE (Aug 25): the one-request fix already went in on Aug 24, and runs now take about 85 seconds against a 15-minute limit
  • The last missing piece was simply reporting how long each run took — added, along with a warning if a run ever creeps past ten minutes
  • Because of that, looking further ahead is now cheap: 45 days holds 100 bookings against 59 for two weeks, not three times as many
Technical notes

The Registrar reads the Skedda board one day at a time — 15 round-trips per pass, and it already hit a wall

Problem

The Registrar's first unattended pass timed out, 2026-08-20 02:00Z, five minutes after it was

switched on. HTTP 504, Cloud Scheduler DEADLINE_EXCEEDED at 299.97s against a 300s ceiling.

The ceiling had no margin in it: Cloud Run's request timeout and the Scheduler attempt deadline were

both 300s, so the first limit hit was also the last. Raised to 900s on both (Junyan's call,

same night — event-gate-00049-dc4), which unblocks tonight and buys roughly 3× headroom. That is a

tuning fix, not a repair.

The actual cost is structural. run() walks the horizon a day at a time:

``python

for offset in range(horizon + 1):

bookings.extend(skedda_read.bookings_on((now + timedelta(days=offset)).date()))

`

With HORIZON_DAYS_DEFAULT = 14 that is 15 sequential HTTP round-trips before a single booking is

processed, and every one of them pays the full latency. Per-booking Notion and alarm calls then run on

top, also serially. Observed pass durations the night it shipped: 146s dry → 239s dry → over 300s

live. A live pass is slower than a dry one because it writes.

Why 900s does not close this. Pass duration scales with the board, and the board grows. The lane

runs every 30 minutes, so the real ceiling is 1800s no matter what the timeout says — past that, passes

overlap, and overlap breaks the single-writer constraint that 079 learned the hard way from the

Codex collision (two actors on the same instruction each verify their own write as correct, and nothing

detects it). This ticket exists so the next wall is seen coming rather than discovered in production.

Shape (not ruled)

  • - The obvious one: read a date RANGE, not a day. skedda-mcp already exposes list_bookings_range,

and /bookingslists itself takes start/end — bookings_on() builds a one-day window out of a

two-sided parameter it already has. One request instead of 15 is most of the win for very little code.

Confirm the response shape and paging behaviour over a 14-day span before assuming it is a drop-in;

the venueusers side-list must still arrive whole, or holder resolution silently degrades to the

stripped-read signature.

  • - Concurrency on the per-booking calls, if the range read alone is not enough. Bounded — the alarm panel

is not something to hammer.

  • - Whatever is done, keep the stripped-session guard intact. It judges holders across the WHOLE read;

a change that splits or streams the read must not let it judge a fragment.

Acceptance

  • - A live pass over the full 14-day horizon completes with wide margin (target: well under 300s, so the

original ceiling would not have been hit).

  • - SWEEP_HORIZON_DAYS stays at 14 — the fix is speed, not seeing less. Shrinking the horizon was

explicitly considered and declined on 2026-08-20, because bookings 8–14 days out would get no

prerequisites until they drifted closer.

  • - Pass duration is reported per pass, so the next approach to the wall is visible before it arrives —

this pairs with 086, which is where a duration would be recorded.

  • - RUNTIMES.md + Plumbing Map entries.

Resolution 2026-08-25 — the structural fix already landed; the missing half was the number

The range read is done. bookings_range(first, last) exists in skedda_read.py and

booking_sweep.run() has called it since 2026-08-24, shipped alongside ticket 090:

`python

return skedda_read.bookings_range(now.date(), (now + timedelta(days=horizon)).date())

`

One /bookingslists request for the whole window, whatever the span. The 15 sequential

round-trips this ticket was written about no longer happen, and the horizon stopped being a

budget question. Measured on 2026-08-25, ten consecutive live passes ran 68–96s against a

900s ceiling — the acceptance target ("well under 300s") is met with 3× margin, and

SWEEP_HORIZON_DAYS stayed at 14 exactly as required.

What was still missing: the duration itself. The acceptance asked for pass duration to be

reported per pass, and it was not. Without it the next wall gets discovered the way the first

one was — a 504 at 02:00. Shipped in 3b1c3563:

  • - every summary now carries duration_s and horizon_days (duration is meaningless without

knowing how far the pass was looking);

  • - a pass over SLOW_PASS_S = 600 logs SLOW PASS explicitly. 600s is a third of the real

ceiling, which is the 1800s cadence rather than the 900s timeout: past 1800s two passes

overlap and the single-writer constraint breaks (079, the Codex collision).

Consequence for the horizon. Cost now scales with bookings in the window, not days in it.

Counted 2026-08-25: 14 days holds 59 non-recurring bookings, 45 days holds 100 — 1.7×,

not 3.2×. A 45-day pass should land near 150s, well inside every ceiling. SWEEP_HORIZON_DAYS=45

is affordable whenever Junyan wants it; that is 110's promise, not this ticket's, and it stays

his call.

Status: closed. Remaining: deploy 3b1c3563` (blocked on approval at time of writing).

✅#110🔑Give people their door code the moment they book2026-08-25
  • Right now you get your code when the robot's window reaches your booking — about two weeks before
  • That was never a decision; it just fell out of how far ahead the robot looks
  • Junyan's call (Aug 24): the code should be set up the moment somebody books, however far off the booking is
  • Codes do not expire any more, so a code made six months early is simply that person's code
  • The catch: the robot must set it up ONCE and then leave it alone, or it would be re-checking thousands of old bookings every half hour forever
  • The alarm check a week before the booking stays, and that is what catches anything that has gone wrong since
  • DECIDED (Aug 25): looking further ahead than two weeks was considered and turned down — Junyan: the code being ready early is an internal detail, not something a renter ever notices
  • It would have handed a live door code to 17 more people today, for bookings up to six weeks away, some of whom will cancel and never come — and codes do not expire
  • The real win landed anyway: a code is now worked out ONCE and never re-checked, so how far ahead we look is a free choice rather than a cost
Technical notes

Door access is due when someone books, not when the horizon reaches them

Ruling (Junyan, 2026-08-24)

"I want to provision door access the moment someone makes a booking (it's not based on the

horizon)."

Today access is due implicitly, as a side effect of the read window: ensure_access runs on

every booking the sweep can see, so a renter gets their code when the horizon reaches them —

14 days out now, 45 if SWEEP_HORIZON_DAYS is raised, and never until then. That is an

accident of how the board is read, not a decision anybody made.

New rule: access is due on booking. Everything else stays due relative to the session —

board row at T−2, confirmation at T−2. Access is the one step whose clock starts at creation.

Harmless by construction: codes have been permanent since windows were retired

(doorman-access-lifecycle.md), so a code minted six months early is simply that renter's code.

For a returning renter ensure_access returns already and writes nothing. The change only

bites for genuinely new renters, and in their favour — they can be told their code the day they

book instead of a fortnight before they arrive.

Why this is NOT just "widen the horizon"

Widening the read is free — one request, whatever the span (090 step 1, landed). Processing

is not. Per booking per pass the sweep currently costs roughly a Notion artist query, a Notion

page GET, and an _alarm_call({verify_only}). At a 14-day horizon that is a few hundred calls

every 30 minutes. At a year it is thousands, forever, re-checking bookings that were settled

months ago.

So "provision on booking" has to mean provision on FIRST SIGHT, then stop re-checking — not

"read further and check everything every pass."

The build

  1. 1. Widen the read — cheap, already possible.
  2. 2. Provision on first sight of a booking id. Which requires knowing what is new, which is

exactly the snapshot / created high-water mark that 090

step 3 builds. Hence the block: this ticket cannot be done honestly without it.

  1. 3. Stop re-checking settled bookings every pass. A booking whose access step has completed

is not re-verified by the sweep. The safety net already exists and is the right one: the

Doorman preflights access 7 days out, so a code that rots between booking and session is

caught near the session, by the job that owns arrival — not by the sweep grinding the panel

every 30 minutes for a year.

Watch for

  • - The recurrence trap. Series records arrive stamped with the SERIES start, so "first sight"

on a recurring booking is not a real arrival. The recurring skip stays (map ruling 2026-08-19).

  • - Losing the snapshot must not mean re-provisioning everybody. It should read as "seen

before" by default — ensure_access returning already makes that safe, but the pass should

not treat a lost snapshot as "everything is new" and stampede the panel.

  • - A booking moved after provisioning does not need a new code (codes are permanent), so

change detection does not have to re-trigger access — only the board row and confirmation.

Ruling 2026-08-25 — the horizon STAYS at 14. This ticket is complete as shipped.

Junyan, asked whether to widen SWEEP_HORIZON_DAYS: *"Code is ready the moment people book is

not actually important it's just an internal process. Let's leave it at 14 then."*

Decided, not deferred. Do not re-open this as an unfinished switch.

The reasoning it rests on, so a later session does not re-litigate it from scratch:

  • - The renter experiences nothing either way. The code reaches them inside the HTA at T−24

regardless of horizon. Provisioning at T−45 versus T−14 changes no message and no timing —

it is bookkeeping, which is exactly what Junyan called it.

  • - Codes are permanent. This service's own cancellation path says so ("the alarm code is

left alone — codes are permanent and this renter may return"). Widening to 45 would have

provisioned 17 additional people who appear nowhere in the next two weeks (counted on

the live board 2026-08-25: the 15–45 day stretch holds 39 non-recurring bookings from 24

holders, 17 of them not seen inside 14 days). More live door codes, longer exposure, higher

cancel-and-never-return rate, for no renter-visible gain.

  • - Cost was never the blocker. The one-request range read landed 2026-08-24 (087), so a

45-day pass would have cost ~1.7× a 14-day one, not 3×. Widening was affordable and was

declined on policy, not budget. Record it that way.

What this ticket actually delivered, and it is the whole win: access no longer depends on

the horizon arriving. access:settled carries a completed access step forward in the

snapshot, so a booking's code is minted once and never re-checked — the per-pass alarm call

per booking, forever, that made any wide horizon impossible. The horizon is now a free policy

dial. Junyan has set it to 14.

Status: closed.

Technical notes

A mirrored platform booking is invisible to the Registrar — "no holder on the booking" drops it silently

Found by the first real one, 2026-08-25 (Raquel K.)

Peerspace request 16:56 EDT → Junyan accepted 16:57 → the mirror created Skedda booking

119728204 at 16:59:06 EDT (POST /mirror-booking 200; booking stamped 20:59:10Z), titled

"Raquel K. Peerspace", Studio 901, 19:00–20:30, $60. That half worked, unattended, two minutes

after the tap — 109's acceptance is met.

Then nothing. The 17:00 and 17:30 sweep passes both skipped her, and skipped her silently:

``python

holder = (booking.get("holder") or "").strip().lower()

if not holder:

return "no holder on the booking"

`

The mirror deliberately creates a booking with no linked Skedda user — Junyan's 2026-08-24

ruling, because creating one emails the guest a sign-up invite they never asked for. The

Registrar's first gate drops exactly that shape. The two lanes were built to the same ruling and

do not join.

Everything a renter actually receives was then done by hand: phone asked for in the Peerspace

chat (17:41), panel user 248361767 + code 7368 created and verified (17:47), HTA sent by

direct SMS (17:53, phone.com 258793424) — 67 minutes before her booking. It worked because

somebody was awake. That is the Julia sentence and the Gabrielle sentence, one lane further along.

Note what this is NOT. It is not the same-day send hold: the Registrar never held her at all,

so there was no queued pair to release. Clearing SWEEP_SAMEDAY_HOLD would not have sent her

anything.

Why the skip is defensible and still wrong

Per booking, "no holder" genuinely means the holder is absent. Across a WHOLE read it means a

stripped session, and run() correctly fails closed on that. The bug is that a mirrored booking

is a known, expected, titled shape — the title convention exists precisely so downstream lanes

can identify the guest without an account — and the sweep treats it as an anonymous nobody.

Worse: a skip files no residue. The lane's whole design principle is that failures are late and

noisy rather than silent and permanent, and this one is silent.

Shape (not ruled)

  • - Recognise the mirror's title convention (Name Platform, e.g. "Raquel K. Peerspace") and treat

the parsed name as the holder for artist matching, instead of skipping. The convention was fixed

for exactly this purpose (084).

  • - The phone is the genuinely missing fact — platforms mask it permanently, and the code must never

go through a platform relay (HOUSE-RULES 🌍 3). So the honest ceiling is: card + code + board row

automatically, and a loud residue naming "ask this guest for a phone number in the platform

chat" — not silence.

  • - Cheapest interim, if the above is not built: make the holder-less skip file a residue when the

title matches the mirror convention. One row, and nobody arrives uncontacted.

Acceptance

  • - A mirrored booking produces either a completed chain or a visible row. Never a silent skip.
  • - Replay Raquel: same board, same result, no human in the loop up to the phone request.
  • - RUNTIMES.md + Plumbing Map entries.

FIXED + DEPLOYED 2026-08-25 evening — the platform two-gate lane

Built and live the same night the gap was found. Verified from the workspace: 27bfa058 is an

ancestor of the running 4da13b48 (/health, started 23:28Z), so all three commits are deployed.

The skip is gone. A mirrored booking is now recognised by its title convention

(platform_name() strips the platform word off "Raquel K. Peerspace") and routed through two

gates instead of being dropped:

  • - GATE 1 — accepted, no phone yet. One keyed ✅ Actions row, one ops text to the on-call phone,

and the phone-number request drafted into the Message Queue (PHONE-{platform}-{stamp},

Channel Platform Relay, Pending Review) in Junyan's own verbatim wording. The chain

deliberately does NOT run: no junk empty-named Artist card, and nothing to send anyway because

the code must never travel through a platform relay (HOUSE-RULES 🌍 3). It rides on the

residue's created so it queues exactly once.

  • - GATE 2 — a phone exists. Exactly one Artist card matching the title-name carries a Phone →

the next pass runs the full normal chain with that identity, and the HTA pulls the phone from

the card like any other renter. Zero or multiple matches both fall back to gate 1 — that

costs a reminder, never a wrong send.

Also ported in the same commit: SameDay.gs's lost contract, *every same-day booking produces one

text, success or failure*. _ops_report()` texts the on-call phone when a same-day booking had real

work done that pass; settled passes stay silent, so the 30-minute cadence cannot text twice about

the same booking. 546 tests pass.

One seam left, named honestly and not a defect: the reply carrying the number is not parsed.

Gate 2 fires on a Phone written to the Artist card, so the number still moves from the relay

reply onto the card by hand. Everything either side of that is automatic — the ask is drafted

without being asked for, and the whole chain runs the moment the number lands.

Note on Junyan's framing ("the Registrar gets the information directly from the mail"): the

platform's mail is where the request goes OUT and where the answer comes BACK, and the outbound

half is now drafted automatically. The inbound number still needs a hand. Recorded precisely so a

later session does not assume an auto-parse that isn't there.

Status: closed. Acceptance replay: the next accepted platform booking should produce a gate-1

row, an ops text, and a drafted ask — with no silent skip anywhere.

✅#084🏷️Bookings from Giggster and Peerspace made more than 2 days ahead reach nobody2026-08-24
  • When someone books through Giggster or Peerspace, the booking appears on our calendar with no person attached — the name is only in the booking's title, like 'Chris Giggster'
  • The fast robot that handles these only wakes up for bookings happening today or tomorrow. Chris booked ten days ahead, so it never fired
  • And the new booking program skips them, because to it they look like a booking with nobody on it
  • So nobody automated touches them at all — 5 out of 31 bookings in the four days we checked
  • What needs deciding: who owns them, whether reading the name out of the title is safe enough, and what to do about the missing phone number
Technical notes

Platform bookings made more than 2 days out reach no automation at all

Question

Found 2026-08-19 while probing the Skedda admin read for 081.

A Giggster/Peerspace/Tagvenue booking is mirrored into Skedda as a casual booking with

no holder — user: {id: null, name: null, email: null, phone: null}. The renter's identity

lives in the booking title, by convention: Chris Giggster, Hanano I. Giggster,

Jessica T. Peerspace, Angelica B. Peerspace, Anneka K. Peerspace.

Nobody automated handles these unless they start today or tomorrow.

  • - send-engine/SameDay.gs has full platform logic — it mirrors the booking, creates the Artist

page, provisions and verifies the code, seeds the board row — but it is mail-triggered at

arrival and only acts when the booking starts today or tomorrow. Chris Giggster was booked

Aug 11 for Aug 21: ten days out, so the lane never fired.

  • - The booking sweep skips them, correctly under its current rules, as "no holder on the booking".

Measured: 5 of 31 bookings in the Aug 20–23 window were platform mirrors.

What has to be decided

  1. 1. Who owns them — the sweep (parse the holder out of the title), a widened same-day lane

(drop its today/tomorrow gate for platform entries only), or a third lane. Note the

single-writer rule from 079: exactly one unattended provisioner.

  1. 2. The title convention as an identity source. "<First> <LastInitial>. <Platform>" is a

human habit, not a schema. What happens when someone types it differently — refuse, or

fuzzy-match and raise residue? (081's rule is: never merge on a name alone.)

  1. 3. The missing phone number. Platform renters have no phone on the Skedda side; Giggster

gives a first name and Peerspace masks the surname. SameDay's existing answer is to do

everything except the send and escalate asking for a mobile number on the platform thread —

is that still right at T−10 rather than T−1, where there is time to ask?

  1. 4. Whether repeat platform renters short-circuit — SameDay already completes fully and

automatically for a platform renter already in the Artist DB with a phone. Same here?

Why it is not just "fold it into the sweep"

The entry shape is genuinely different — no holder record, no phone, identity by title

convention, and a mirror that may itself need creating. The same-day lane already solved most

of this once; the decision is whether to reuse that code by widening its window or to

re-implement in the sweep, and that is a design call, not a build detail.

---

2026-08-19, after the one-owner ruling: question 1 (who owns them) is now answered in

principle — 089 one job, two triggers makes the sweep the sole

owner, and platform-mirror creation moves into it (089 §What moves, item 3). Questions 2–4

(title-convention identity, the missing phone at T−10, repeat-renter short-circuit) remain

this ticket's grilling.

---

New evidence, 2026-08-24 — the mirror itself is manual, and it can be a day late

This ticket assumed a platform booking is mirrored into Skedda ("mirrored as a casual

booking with no holder") and that the open question was who handles it afterwards. Julia S.

shows the mirror is not happening on its own either — Junyan does it by hand, and when he

isn't watching, the room is not held at all.

Julia S., Peerspace, Sun 23 Aug 19:00–21:00, 509A, $64, "Me again! Just practice."

| When (UTC) | What arrived / happened |

|---|---|

| 23 Aug 04:26 | "Julia S. sent you a booking request" |

| 23 Aug 05:50 | "Your booking is confirmed with Julia S." — the class sdParsePeerspace_ matches. 01:50 Toronto, ~17h before the session |

| 23 Aug 22:29 | "starts soon" reminder, 31 min before start — labelled Artist Care - Booking Coming Up. A label, not an action |

| 24 Aug 01:04 | "booking is complete" |

| 24 Aug 15:40 | Skedda booking 119616803 "Julia S. Peerspace FBS" created — a day after the session ended |

So for the whole session the room was not on our calendar. Not "no board row" — no

booking. Anyone could have booked 509A over her. She is also a returning Peerspace renter

(previous booking 15 Aug), and Peerspace masks her number, so nothing could have texted her

anyway.

Compare Kim M. (Peerspace, mail 15 Aug 16:17Z → mirror 119143355 created 16:24Z, seven

minutes later, by hand). Same path, and it worked — because Junyan happened to be at the desk.

Julia's landed at 01:50 Toronto. That is the whole difference between the two outcomes.

What this fixes in the ticket's framing

  • - Question 1 ("who owns them") had been answered in principle by 089.

But 089 and 108 both work off the Skedda board,

and a platform booking is not on it until something mirrors it. Neither ticket helps Julia.

The mirror is upstream of everything this map has built.

  • - The route already exists and was proven live 2026-08-12: POST /mirror-booking on skedda-mcp

(skedda-mcp-00020-bxk), create + read-back verified, idempotent on a repeat call. What is

missing is the thing that calls it — that logic lives only in the uninstalled SameDay.gs.

  • - So the cheapest real fix is a small deterministic caller on the platform confirmation mail:

no LLM, no send authority, one job — hold the room and attach the holder. It would have

created Julia's booking at 01:50.

Still open (unchanged, and now sharper)

Q2 title-convention identity, Q3 the missing phone, Q4 repeat-renter short-circuit. Julia

bears on Q4 directly: she is a repeat renter whose number we still do not have, because

Peerspace masks it on every booking, not just the first.

Live and unhandled right now

Kim M., Thu 27 Aug 18:30–21:30, Studio 693, 30 attendees. Mirrored (so the room is held)

but holder null and no phone — invisible to every robot. Junyan asked her for a number on

21 Aug; no reply on the thread as of 24 Aug. Three days out.

Correction, same day — most of the downstream IS handled

The section above claimed Kim M. was "invisible to every robot" and that Julia got nothing.

Both wrong, checked against live records 2026-08-24:

  • - Kim M. has an Artist card keyed on her relay, alarm code 6205 verified live on 4 panels

normally.

  • - ~20 Peerspace guests hold cards on their relay address, each with a code and an

Alarm Display Name. This route has been working for months.

Why: the FBS board is built from the Skedda calendar by booking title, not by holder

record. So a blank-holder mirror is legible to the desk. The sweep's holder-based lookup is the

only thing that skips them, and the board row is what actually drives EOB/PBF.

So the ticket narrows sharply. The failure is one step: nothing puts the booking on the

calendar. Everything after that already works. Q1 (who owns them) and Q4 (repeat-renter

short-circuit) are effectively answered by observation — the existing path handles both.

Junyan's ruling, 2026-08-24: platform bookings stay casual bookings with no Skedda user.

Do not create Skedda accounts for platform guests — confirmed against prior bookings, which are

all type: 1, user: null. This is now settled, not open. Note the practical reason found while

testing: create_user emails the person a Skedda registration invite on commit, which no

platform guest should receive.

Q2 (title-convention identity) — RULED (Junyan, 2026-08-24): the title is <Name> <Platform>.

e.g. Kim M. Peerspace. It is load-bearing precisely because the holder is null by design, so

this string is the only link from a calendar booking to a person. Variants still in the wild

(Julia S. Peerspace FBS, Peerspace Booking, Julia S.) normalise on touch. All four of this

ticket's questions are now answered — Q1 and Q4 by observation, Q2 and the casual-booking rule

problem below, which is a different ticket's shape.

permanently. Reply on the platform thread. Not worth further design.

New, real, and not previously on this ticket: one card per guest. Peerspace issues a fresh

relay per booking, so every booking mints a new Artist card. Julia S. has two (relays

prod-6a5753e7… and prod-6a8088af…, same phone, same code 9312). The contact-points table

(101/103) exists for

exactly this and should absorb platform relays.

Closed 2026-08-24 — all four questions answered

  • - Q4 repeat-renter short-circuit — answered by observation: repeat platform renters already

the guest a registration invite, which settles it independently of preference.

The one-card-per-guest problem is NOT a new ticket — 101

✅#107🕳️Found the hole that made a last-minute booking vanish2026-08-21
  • Sara booked studio 509B the night before and got nothing back — no code, no board row, no message
  • The booking robot saw it and stepped aside for a helper it thought owned last-minute bookings
  • That helper was written back in August but never actually turned on, so nothing ever picked the booking up
  • Every last-minute booking since Aug 20 was handed to nobody, quietly
  • The answer: stop stepping aside at all, rather than waiting a while and then stepping in
  • We are NOT switching the old helper on — it is allowed to text door codes on its own, and it is being removed anyway
Technical notes

Instant bookings fall into the Registrar/same-day-lane deference gap

The defect

Skedda instant booking (approval off, enabled by Junyan 2026-08-21) produces the

self-serve notification class — "X made a booking" — for EVERY direct booking,

new renters included. Two consumers each assume the other owns it:

  • - SameDay.gs (send-engine) does not fire on that class — it watched the

confirmation/approval classes that instant booking just abolished for direct

bookings. (Same root cause as the Desiree Joy miss: "the booking-request

approval requirement was removed, ending the notification class that fed

Sales - Booking Request.")

  • - The Registrar (booking_sweep) sees the booking but defers anything in the

today/tomorrow window: deferred: "same-day lane owns this booking" — to a

lane that is not coming. The deferral never expires.

Result: a new-renter instant booking inside the window gets NO card, NO board

row, NO confirmation, NO HTA, NO code check — silently, forever.

Proven case

Sara Aljabery, booked 2026-08-21 21:59Z for Aug 22 13:15 (509B). Dry-run showed

artist:would-create, access:check-failed:SweepError, deferred on every pass

for ~1h. Unstuck attended 2026-08-21 ~23:05Z (card linked, code verified,

confirmation + Returning Access sent, receipts phonecom:258508808/258508796,

Sales ticket at Review). JOB 2's Skedda-label branch also calls this class

"self-serve calendar noise — mark read only", so no LLM pass catches it either.

The fix to design (pick one, or both)

  1. 1. Teach SameDay.gs the "made a booking" class (it already handles the platform

accept class), OR

  1. 2. Make the Registrar's deference expire: if the booking is inside the window

AND the lane has produced nothing (no board row / no HTA-sameday queue row)

after N minutes, the sweep takes ownership instead of deferring again.

Expiring deference is the more robust shape — it also covers the lane being

down, not just blind.

Also update JOB 2's "self-serve calendar noise" line: noise only when the

holder is a KNOWN artist; unknown holder → last-minute path. (The 2026-08-21

canon narrowing covered platform enquiries; this is the Skedda-direct twin.)

Constraints

  • - One writer per booking stays the rule — the fix must not create double-sends.
  • - Production trigger/config changes need Junyan's explicit approval.

---

Resolution (2026-08-21)

The stated cause #1 is wrong, and the correction changes the fix.

send-engine/SameDay.gs already handles this exact class. Line 152:

subject.match(/^(.+?) made a booking for /) → kind = 'direct', and RUNTIMES.md's

lane table lists "X made a booking" as a detected class. Sara Aljabery's mail

(thread 1a02656427786670, 2026-08-21 21:59:36Z, subject "Sara Aljabery made a

booking for Saturday, August 22, 2026, 1:15 p.m. (Studio 509B)") parses, is not

staff, is not staff-made, and starts tomorrow — it qualifies under

sdQualifies_. Teaching SameDay.gs this class would have been a no-op.

The actual cause: the lane SameDay.gs is deferred to has never been installed.

RUNTIMES.md, ⚠️ block under "Apps Script — Same-Day Access Lane": *"The Apps Script

half (SameDay.gs) is not installed yet — paste, properties, DB sharing, timezone,

dry run, triggers. The lane cannot fire until SAMEDAY_ENABLED=true."* Restated

2026-08-21 under DocIntake: *"two uninstalled lanes now sit in this project

(SameDay.gs is also pending)."* Built 2026-08-12, never turned on.

So the Registrar (live 2026-08-20) shipped a deference branch pointing at a lane

that has never run. deferred: "same-day lane owns this booking" has been false

for every same-day booking since the sweep went live. Instant booking did not

create this hole — it removed the last accidental cover for it (the approval class

that fed Sales - Booking Request, which a human worked by hand).

Decision: option 2, in its strongest form — delete the deference, do not expire it.

Expiring deference is a hedge against a lane being down. A lane that was never

installed is down 100% of the time, so a timer only buys N minutes of guaranteed

nothing on every same-day booking — the worst window to spend latency in. The sweep

takes the window unconditionally.

Option 1 is rejected: installing SameDay.gs now means standing up the desk's only

unattended auto-send-a-code-to-an-unverified-first-timer grant, a week before

089 — one job, two triggers retires it. Never install

a lane you are about to remove.

Shipped in two stages, so the hole closes without moving the send grant:

  • - Stage A — record-only takeover, no new send authority. Cut as

108. Inside the window the sweep does

exactly what it already does outside it — artist record, verified alarm access,

board row, confirmation queued at Pending Review — and instead of the same-day HTA

files a loud Actions row + Ops Lead SMS: "same-day booking, HTA owed, human sends."

Nothing new is authorized to send; the silent-forever failure becomes a visible,

attended one. This is the Sara case handled by the machine up to the point a human

is genuinely needed.

  • - Stage B — 089 as already ruled. The imminent branch, canonical HTA templating,

and SameDay's auto-send bounds move into the sweep; SameDay.gs is marked

do-not-install rather than retired-after-parallel-week (there is no lane to run in

parallel with — 089's "parallel week" precondition is void, and 089 should say so).

One-writer safety. The rule holds trivially at Stage A: with SameDay.gs

uninstalled there is no second writer. If it is ever installed, the guard is the

sweep's existing per-booking dedup (board row / queue row re-derived each pass), not

a deference rule — which is exactly 089's "same code path on both triggers, no seam

to defend."

Canon defects this exposes, fixed with Stage A (not optional — they are what made

the gap invisible):

  1. 1. dc-canon/services/event-gate/booking_sweep.py lines 25–29 assert SameDay.gs

"already owns bookings that start today or tomorrow, mail-triggered and acting

within a minute." False since the sweep shipped. The docstring is the reason no

one looked.

  1. 2. RUNTIMES.md lines 69–70 and the booking-sweep row repeat the same claim.
  2. 3. JOB 2's Skedda-label branch: "self-serve calendar noise — mark read only" is

noise only when the holder is a known artist; unknown holder → last-minute

path. (Twin of the 2026-08-21 platform-enquiry narrowing.)

Junyan's call needed before anything ships (production trigger/config): approve

Stage A, and confirm SameDay.gs stays uninstalled through 089 rather than being

switched on as a stopgap.

✅#067📬An email that lands in junk was lost to our records forever2026-08-20
  • DONE. Fixed August 20
  • What went wrong: on August 6 a renter emailed asking for door codes. Our record book has Junyan's reply but not her question, so the conversation reads as if the desk wrote to her out of nowhere
  • Why: the collector only ever looks forward. It reads new mail since the last time it looked, then moves its bookmark past that point and never looks back
  • So a message hidden at the moment it looked — sitting in the junk folder, most likely — is missed, and stays missed. Even when a person later says 'this is not junk', the bookmark has already moved past it. Nothing goes back for it
  • The fix: once a day the collector re-reads the last week and files anything it does not already have. Any kind of miss now heals itself, not just junk ones
  • We deliberately did NOT make it read the junk folder. Mail sitting in junk is junk and stays out of the record. But the moment a person rescues it, the next day's re-read picks it up. The person's click is the repair
  • It says so out loud when it recovers something — because a quiet fix would hide the fact that the first pass has a blind spot
  • Proved by doing it: we deleted a three-day-old message from the records and the next run put it back, identical, and said so
  • One thing we could not prove: that junk really was the cause that day. It does not matter — the fix repairs any kind of miss, and the cause only mattered for the option we did not choose
Technical notes

Customer mail that lands in spam never reaches canon — even after a human rescues it

Question

Found while checking 062's backfill. Camille Quesada emailed the desk 2026-08-06 19:32Z

("Booking for Sunday, August 9th" — asking for door codes and alarm instructions). Junyan's

Aug-7 reply reached canon; her original never did, so the thread read as the desk writing

unprompted. Every other turn across Aug 5–7 was present — SMS matched 200/200 against Phone.com,

and 14 of 15 checked Gmail turns were in the ledger. This was the only hole, and it was not

caused by the outage: the poller's cursor rides in the same commit as the messages, so an

outage-lost message re-captures on the next tick. This one was skipped, not lost.

Most likely mechanism (not yet proven): the message was in spam when the poller ran.

gmail_pull.py lists with includeSpamTrash: false and only ever looks forward from its cursor

(after:{cursor - 3600}). So a message quarantined at poll time is invisible, and staying

invisible is permanent — a human un-spamming it later changes nothing, because the cursor has

already moved past that hour. Her thread carries no labelIds at all, consistent with a

message that was rescued from spam rather than filed by a normal Gmail filter.

Backfilled by hand: messages/2026/08/06/gmail-19fd88ffa03e12ca.json (commit c80c0f99,

source: backfill-gmail-poller-gap), and the thread now renders correctly.

Decide the fix:

  1. 1. Confirm the mechanism first. Is spam the actual cause? Check whether Gmail still shows

any spam-history signal for that message, and test the poller against a deliberately

spam-filed message before designing around the theory.

  1. 2. Then choose the repair. Options seen so far: poll with includeSpamTrash: true and drop

true spam by rule in code (the ledger already enforces ledger_exclude.json that way); or

add a slower, wider re-scan pass (e.g. once a day over the last 7 days, dedup by filename)

so any forward-cursor skip self-heals rather than becoming permanent.

  1. 3. Consider the class, not just the cause. A cursor that only moves forward turns every

kind of skip into a permanent hole. The re-scan option fixes the class; the spam flag fixes

one instance.

Acceptance

  • - Mechanism confirmed or refuted with a real test, not inference.
  • - A message the poller skipped on its first pass demonstrably reaches canon on a later one.

---

RESOLVED 2026-08-20 — fix the class, not the instance

Chosen: option 2, the wide re-scan. Option 1 (includeSpamTrash: true) is explicitly NOT taken,

and the reason is the part worth keeping: leaving the poller on includeSpamTrash: false means a

message still sitting in spam is spam and stays out of canon, while a human clicking "Not spam" is

itself the repair — the next re-scan sees the message and files it. That gives the rescue path for

free, with no spam-dropping rule to maintain and no junk entering the ledger. Flipping the flag would

have bought the same recovery and handed us a second exclusion list to keep correct forever.

Shipped in scripts/gmail_pull.py (dc-canon 8e79f74e): once every 24h the poller re-lists the last

7 days with the same query and the same flag, and writes whatever canon is missing. Three properties

were designed for and then tested rather than assumed:

  • - The re-scan never moves the forward cursor. It reads old mail by definition; letting it touch

last_epoch would be a way to skip live messages — the defect, rebuilt.

  • - It is loud. A recovery prints ::warning:: naming the count. A re-scan that healed quietly

would hide the fact that the forward pass has a blind spot, which is the thing actually worth

knowing. A clean pass prints one line saying so.

  • - It is nearly free. The existing one-tree-scan have set means an already-captured message costs

a dict lookup and no messages.get call, so a week of re-listing is a handful of list calls and

zero writes in steady state. It is gated to once a day anyway — no reason to pay it 60× on a 15-min

tick.

Acceptance — met, by running it

Against the live mailbox from the full desk-store checkout, cursor left at its real value:

  1. 1. Deleted messages/2026/08/17/gmail-1a00daea4af361ec.json from canon — a message ~3 days old and

far behind the forward cursor, i.e. exactly the ticket's situation.

  1. 2. Ran the poller. The forward pass did not see it (correct — the cursor is past it). The re-scan

recovered it, named it, and raised the warning. The recovered file is byte-identical to the

original: it does not appear in the staged diff at all. Only the state file changed, and only to

add last_rescan_epoch — last_epoch was untouched.

  1. 3. Re-ran immediately: no re-scan (24h gate holds). Forced it due: 7-day rescan clean — nothing

missing, zero writes. Worktree restored to clean.

That is the acceptance line satisfied literally: *a message the poller skipped on its first pass

demonstrably reaches canon on a later one.*

Mechanism — partly confirmed, and deliberately not load-bearing

Tested rather than inferred, against the live mailbox:

  • - Camille's message is visible to the poller's exact query today, under both includeSpamTrash

values (79-message window, present in both). Nothing about the message excludes it permanently —

it was invisible only at poll time.

  • - Its labels are ['CATEGORY_PERSONAL'] — no INBOX, no SPAM. Archived.
  • - A spam round-trip is label-destructive: marking a live message SPAM and removing SPAM stripped

IMPORTANT and CATEGORY_PERSONAL, leaving only the user label. That is consistent with the

ticket's "carries no labelIds at all" observation for a rescued message, and it also means

labels cannot be used as evidence of a message's spam history — the round-trip erases the trace.

Worth knowing before anyone tries to audit this class from labels again.

  • - The controlled test was NOT completed. Marking a message SPAM and re-listing under both flag

values was set up twice and blocked both times by the sandbox's command classifier, so

"spam specifically, at 19:32Z on 2026-08-06" remains unproven. The mailbox was left as found

(SPAM/TRASH removed from both test messages — noted below).

This gap does not block the decision, and that is the finding. The re-scan is

mechanism-independent: it heals a skip whatever caused it — spam, an API blip, a mid-page failure,

a delayed delivery whose internalDate lands behind the cursor. The mechanism question was only ever

load-bearing for option 1, which is the option not taken. Chasing the proof further would have

sharpened an answer nothing now depends on.

Two live-mailbox side effects, disclosed

Both test messages were restored, but a trash/spam round-trip does not restore system labels:

  • - 1a01527a0a544380 (Toronto Hydro bill notice, one of six identical copies) — trashed and restored;

lost CATEGORY_UPDATES. untrash_message returned "The caller does not have permission"; removing

the TRASH label directly worked. Worth knowing for any future session: the business Gmail

connector can trash but cannot untrash — use unlabel_message with TRASH.

  • - 1a0192809a4993cb (Tagvenue solicitation) — SPAM applied and removed; lost IMPORTANT and

CATEGORY_PERSONAL, kept its user label.

Left open

  • - The re-scan window is 7 days. A hole older than that at the moment it is created still needs a

hand backfill via --since. Seven days was chosen because it comfortably covers "a human noticed

over the weekend"; nothing about the design stops it being widened if a real case exceeds it.

  • - The re-scan only ever runs when the poller runs. If the 15-min tick itself stalls for days,

nothing re-scans either — that silence is the run-error/monitor path's job, not this one's.

✅#082🚀The Registrar is switched on2026-08-20
  • DONE. It runs by itself every half hour now, around the clock, starting tonight
  • What it does: it reads the booking calendar and, for every new direct booking, sets up whatever is missing — the renter's record, a working door code, the board row two days before, the confirmation message waiting for approval
  • It never sends anything and never touches money. Confirmations sit in the queue until a person releases them
  • It has no memory of what it already did, and that is on purpose. Every half hour it just looks at what should exist by now and makes it so. Skip a pass, nothing is lost. Run it twice, nothing happens twice
  • It does not read email at all. The 'has this been dealt with' mark on those emails turned out to be untrustworthy, so it reads the calendar directly instead
  • The last practice pass before switching on was clean: 165 bookings looked at, nothing left needing a person, no errors
  • That same pass on the older version had left one note about a renter it could not find. The name fix from the other ticket resolved her. Good proof the names really were the problem
  • To stop it, pause the half-hourly job. That is the fast, reversible way
  • Still worth watching this first day: whether the notes it leaves for Junyan read like something a person can act on, and whether there are a few or a wall of them. No test can answer that
Technical notes

Ship The Registrar (the booking sweep) — deploy, dry-run against the live board, then schedule it

Question

081 built the lane and left it unshipped on purpose: it writes

to Alarm.com, and flipping on a production trigger is Junyan's call, never an agent's. This

ticket is that flip, done in the order that makes a bad pass visible before it is an unattended

one.

  1. 1. Deploy the event-gate revision carrying booking_sweep.py (the route is inert until then).

Set BOOKING_SWEEP_DISABLED=1 in the same deploy so the first live revision cannot act.

(already there for the alarm watch), SKEDDA_COOKIE or the Secret Manager fallback,

NOTION_TOKEN.

  1. 3. POST /booking-sweep/<token>?dry_run=true by hand. Read the acted list against the real

Skedda board: the skip classes must be the staff bookings and nothing else, the would-

Progress — steps 1–3 DONE (recorded 2026-08-19, from another session's live work)

Found already done in the working tree, unrecorded on this ticket:

  • - Deployed and dry-run against the live board. The pass ran for real.
  • - It found a bug that only a live pass could find. The sweep asked the panel using the

Skedda holder string, but the panel is keyed on the Artist row's Alarm Display Name

— the two differ routinely ("Dee Brown" vs "Dee Adelubi Brown", "Kelly-Ann Johnson (Private

Classes)" vs "KellyAnn Johnson", married names). Asking for the holder returns no match,

which reads exactly like "no access" and mints a residue row about a renter whose code has

worked for months. Four of four residue rows in the first live pass were this bug.

  • - Fixed and covered by tests: the artist page is now read before the access branch and

the panel is asked under Alarm Display Name, falling back to the holder only when the

Named 2026-08-19: The Registrar

Junyan's pick, house style (definite article + role). Everything it touches is a record being

created — it enters a new renter in the book, issues their code, files the board card, queues

the confirmation. It never speaks to a renter; it works entirely before they exist to the desk,

then hands off to The Doorman, who does the letting-in. That handoff is the reason Porter

and Usher were rejected: both flirt with the Doorman's territory, and two agents whose names

imply the same job is exactly how a seam gets blurred.

Use this name in the 🚥 Run Monitor row, the RUNTIMES.md entry, the Plumbing Map entry, and

its per-pass report line. booking_sweep.py stays the filename.

The Registrar is live. Junyan ran both commands (the classifier blocks agent deploys; that is

the standing convention for this lane, not a workaround). Order held exactly as specified: deploy →

dry-run against the live board → schedule.

| | |

|---|---|

| Revision | event-gate-00047-rvm |

| Scheduler job | booking-sweep, us-central1, /30 * Etc/UTC, deadline 300s, ENABLED |

| First fire | 2026-08-20 02:00Z |

| Release dry pass | 165 scanned · 125 skipped · 10 deferred · 0 residue · 0 errors |

| Suite | 397 |

Two things carried in on the same revision, neither of which had ever run in production:

  1. 1. Ticket 085's identity code. The dry pass on the PREVIOUS revision filed one residue row

(Vanessa Zavatti, 509A, no Artist row and no panel user); the same pass on 00047 filed zero.

The id-first lookup resolved what the name join could not — the cleanest evidence yet that the

defect was the join and not the renter.

  1. 2. The Skedda session fix (see below).

The kill switch is NOT set, and this is worth knowing rather than discovering: BOOKING_SWEEP_DISABLED

defaults to off, so there was no switch to flip — the lane went live the moment the scheduler job existed.

Creating the job WAS the release. To stop it, pause the scheduler job (fast, reversible) or set the

variable with --update-env-vars; --set-env-vars would drop the other 14 and cripple the service.

The defect, fixed on the way out the door

skedda_read.py cached the anti-forgery token in a bare process global. The token is minted for a

session, so a warm instance holding one across a cookie rotation read anonymously forever — HTTP 200,

holders stripped, nothing raised. Same signature as the 08-11 outage, different door.

  • - The cache is keyed on a sha256 fingerprint of the cookie that produced the token (a rotation

self-invalidates), with a 15-minute TTL (SKEDDA_TOKEN_TTL). The fingerprint is deliberately not the

cookie: that value ends up in comparisons and, one careless log line later, in Cloud Logging.

  • - reset_session_cache() drops token and space names together; the 401/403 path calls it.
  • - The stripped read self-heals — reset, read once more, and only then fail. A second stripped read

still fails loudly and files sweep-blind. The retry must not paper over a genuinely non-admin cookie,

which is a different fault with a different fix.

  • - Six tests: rotation re-scrapes, a stable cookie does not, TTL expiry, the retry, the retry not hiding a

real failure, and the fingerprint never leaking the cookie.

NOT done — the run-monitor row

082's step list called for a 🚥 Run Monitor row and there is none, because the Registrar has

nothing to check in with: it writes on failure and on findings, never on a healthy pass, so a

stall is currently invisible. That needs code, not a row. Cut as

086 rather than left implied — the alternative is a ticket that

reads as fully shipped while the lane's own silence-detection is missing, which is the exact

failure class this effort exists to kill.

Both loose ends closed the same night (Junyan: "let's also fix them now")

1. The kill switch is explicit. BOOKING_SWEEP_DISABLED=0 set on event-gate-00048-nw2 via

--update-env-vars (verified afterwards: 19 variables, all five of the ones --set-env-vars would have

dropped still present). Behaviour is identical — unset and 0 both mean "off" — but it is now visible.

An operator reading the service config previously saw no switch at all and would reach for the scheduler

job without knowing a switch existed. A control nobody can find is not a control.

2. alarm-mcp redeployed — alarm-mcp-00037-9wb, image 0820-eae8f33, 01:47Z. deploy.sh verified the

live tag matches the tag it just built, which is the whole reason that script exists. 085's server-side

resolution proven on the case that started the ticket:

``

check_access_covers("Dee Brown") → manual_no_match

check_access_covers("Dee Brown", artist_page_id=…) → resolved_by: artist_alarm_user_id

→ "Dee Adelubi Brown", 4 panels

`

That is the exact miss that produced four false residue rows and that I then repeated by hand. It no

longer happens. End-to-end dry pass with both services on the 085 code: 166 scanned · 125 skipped ·

11 deferred · 0 residue · 0 errors.

Noticed while probing, filed here rather than fixed: Dee is on a temporary access window (14 Jul →

14 Sep), not permanent. Windows were retired for renters (canon doorman-access-lifecycle.md); this is

the legacy-window migration class the Doorman already sweeps for. Not a Registrar defect, and not mine to

fix tonight.

Left open, deliberately
