Resolution — 2026-08-04

The checker is alive, hosted in the cloud, and now reports its own health.

1. The blackout window

Last clean run 2026-07-29 13:25 -03 (the 🩺 page's own heading — it had been serving that

five-day-old snapshot ever since, complete with 10 defects, reading exactly like a healthy run;

nothing on the page said how old it was). 84 crashed runs followed: 72 × HTTP 400 from the

CC query, and 12 × DNS failure — the laptop asleep or offline, the Mac-bound half of the

problem showing up in the evidence rather than the argument. Nothing else read the page during

the gap that we can attribute, but nothing could have told the difference either — which is

the finding that matters.

2. The query

Not a rename and not a retype: the Customer Care DB's Status property has been deleted

outright. CC now carries Position alone, with the same option set as Sales

(Routing / In Progress / Gate:* / Review / Won / Lost / Done). The query is now the Sales

shape — open = Position not in Won/Lost/Done, Review deliberately still open, because

finishers stop there. Invariant F (Status=Resolved vs Position) is retired: with no

Status property, Position cannot disagree with it.

3. The venue — folded into event-gate (Junyan's ruling)

Same Cloud Run service as 042's ladder: one container, one

NOTION_TOKEN, one deploy, one health route to watch, rather than a second thing that can die

quietly. POST /invariants/<GATE_PATH_TOKEN> (?dry_run=true to read without writing), fired by

its own Cloud Scheduler job *desk-invariants, 25 8-22 America/Toronto. The route is

read-and-report, so the SMS lane's GATE_DISABLED does not gate it — INVARIANTS_DISABLED=1 is

its own kill switch. GitHub Actions stayed disqualified.

4. v1 → v2 — v2 shipped (Junyan's go)

invariants.py in dc-canon/services/event-gate is the v2 port: stable defect keys, run

persistence, 3-consecutive-run escalation to an ✅ Actions row (Raised by = Desk Invariants,

For = Ops Lead), trend history, and invariant I. Three things changed in the move, each because

the cloud is not a Mac:

  • - State lives on the 🩺 page**, as chunked JSON code blocks — Cloud Run instances are

ephemeral, and a bucket would have meant another credential. *(Caught in flight: at 27 defects

the state overran Notion's 2000-char text cap and parsed back as a cold start — run counts

reset every pass, escalation silently never fires. Exactly this ticket's disease. Now chunked,

with history dropped before defects if it ever overflows, and tested.)*

  • - Escalation is capped at 5 rows per run, most-persistent first. A five-day backlog would

otherwise have landed as 27 rows in one pass, which is how a list gets ignored.

  • - Invariant I is skipped, not alarmed, where the J28 ledger is unreachable (INV_LEDGER_URL

unset today); the 🩺 page says so on every run. An unreachable ledger is a venue fact, not a

desk defect. Wiring the ledger URL is the one piece left — see the fog note on the map.

5. Self-health

A run that cannot complete files one Type = Run Error row (Raised by = Desk Invariants,

For = Ops Lead), deduped against the open row — 84 broken runs file one row, not 84, but never

zero. The next clean run marks that row Processed, so a stale alarm can't linger either.

Verified end-to-end against live Notion (filed, deduped, cleaned up). 168 tests green.

Cutover

launchd ca.danceannex.desk-invariants unloaded, plist renamed .RETIRED-2026-08-04, and

both Mac scripts carry 🚦 RETIRED / DO-NOT-RE-ENABLE banners (kept as manual fallback only).

Technical notes

The checker cried wolf — invariant G counted scheduled sends as stuck

Question

Cut 2026-08-04 as *"the send engine is not draining the queue — 16 messages stuck at Ready to

Send"*, on the repaired checker's first live report (043).

That premise was wrong, and the ticket's real subject is the checker. Investigating it first

is what this ticket did; the finding inverted it.

Resolution — 2026-08-04

The send engine is healthy. Invariant G was lying. Three self-inflicted false alarms found

and fixed; the first day's report went from 27 defects to 11, all genuine.

1. G ignored Send After — the queue's scheduled-send feature

The 📤 queue supports scheduled sends: EOB, AVA, and dated payment check-ins sit at

Ready to Send with a future Send After by design. G measured every such row as stuck.

The headline "~10 days stuck" row — Amandeep Singh Bharaj — Aug 6 payment check-in, created

Jul 25 — was correctly waiting for its Aug 6 09:00 date. Nothing needed rescuing; no message

was late; no row needed a human decision about sending it late, which was to be step 1 of

this ticket.

Measured over the 22 scheduled rows dispatched since Aug 3: median lag from Send After to

Sent At = 0.0 minutes. The engine (029) hits its times

exactly. The overnight 2 → 16 climb was the evening's EOB/AVA rows queueing for their slots, and

by morning they had all gone out on schedule.

2. G measured from creation, not release

A row is created at Pending Review and released to Ready to Send later, so age-since-creation

charged human review time (median 64 min) to the engine. Notion records no release timestamp, so

G now measures from Send After when set, else the row's last edit — which is the Status flip

in the ordinary case, and errs safe: a genuinely undispatched row stops being edited, so its age

keeps growing. Threshold raised 10 → 15 minutes, to absorb clock skew and one missed poll.

3. The checker was generating its own defects

Exposed once G went quiet: invariant C flagged the checker's own escalation rows (blank

Gate Type, linked to a gate ticket) — which escalate into more rows, which trip C again. And

the mirror image: invariant B was being silenced by those same rows — "this gate ticket has

no open Action row" was cleared by the row the checker had filed about that very absence, so an

orphaned gate would go quiet forever without a human ever raising the real gate row. Rows with

Raised by = Desk Invariants are now excluded from both.

Shipped: 174 tests green, commit ba978e6e, revision event-gate-00018-hh7.

The lesson worth keeping

A checker that cries wolf is worse than no checker — it teaches the desk to skim past its page,

which is a slower version of the blackout 043 just fixed. 43 of the first 44 alarms were noise

(16 G + 9 C + 2 B suppressions). Any new invariant designed in

044 must be tested against a day of live data before it is

allowed to escalate to a human, and must never count its own output as desk state.

Still standing from the first report: the Studio 693 alarm-trigger ticket (open, untouched

48h+, sitting at Gate: Staff with no action row) and the Aug 1 Studio 509 door-left-open

ticket. Those were real, and they are on the Ops Lead's list.

Graduated as 043.

Rulings

  1. 1. Cloud, not the Mac (Junyan: *"I want to move it to the cloud — everything needs to be in the

cloud"*). The Jul 25 ruling ("the re-check lives in desk_invariants_v2") stands on substance

— deterministic checker, not an LLM pass — but its venue is overruled: a Mac-bound launchd

script is not an acceptable home for a renter-facing deadline check, and v1 just proved why.

  1. 2. Home = the existing event-gate Cloud Run service. Already deployed and healthy, already

ticks every minute on Cloud Scheduler, already reaches the live panel via alarm-mcp

(https://alarm-mcp-cfnon4wh3q-uc.a.run.app), and already carries the only push path at the desk

that has been proven to page a human (escalation.py, ticket 036, live in ~4 min).

GitHub Actions ruled OUT for this job specifically: the map's own scheduler-reliability fog

records Actions cron silently dropping runs (watchlist 3×/12h). A dropped poll is recoverable;

a dropped deadline check is worse than no check, because it reads as coverage.

  1. 3. The invariant: no booking may start while an Access / PIN flag against it is open.

Re-verify against the live panel, never the row status. A passing panel check auto-closes

the flag silently — no human is touched when the system fixes itself.

  1. 4. The ladder (Junyan's shape — starts the day with a human holding it, then escalates *up the

chain*, rather than staying quiet then bursting):

| Rung | When | Action |

|---|---|---|

| 1 | Morning of the booking | The Doorman tells the morning ops person (Ela) at its existing ~06:00 look — it is already awake, already reads the panel, already raises the row |

| 2 | (re-check loop) | event-gate re-verifies open access flags against the live panel every 30 min; silent while it holds |

| 3 | 2h before start, still broken | Direct message to Junyan — a real page, not a queue row |

| 4 | 45 min before start, still broken | Send the Alarm.com Emergency Code user to the renter + file the mandatory reset row |

  1. 5. Doorman split — half yes. Junyan asked whether this is the Doorman's job. Rung 1 yes;

rungs 2–4 no. The Doorman fires 04:00 + ~06:00 and is then asleep — it did its job perfectly

at 06:02 on Jul 24 and the next 13.5 hours had no owner. Waking an LLM pass all day to watch a

clock is expensive work that plain code already does for free on the gate's 1-min tick.

Doorman spots and tells Ela; event-gate does the nagging.

  1. 6. Rung 4 — the emergency code, with the risk named. The safer design (auto-create a time-boxed

per-renter Alarm.com user, as Junyan did by hand on Jul 24: window 19:36 Jul 24 → Aug 24) is

not available: alarm-mcp is read-only by deliberate design (adc_client.py: *"exposes NO

arm / disarm / lock / unlock calls"*), with no user-creation path. Given read-only, the standing

code is the only tool that gets a renter through a door unattended.

What is being sent is a master key, and the build must treat it as one — the Alarm.com

Emergency Code user is Locations: All / Access Period: At All Times /

Access Type: Existing + Future: every studio, no expiry, including studios not yet added.

Junyan accepted this with the rotation guardrail (next ruling).

The code digits are deliberately not recorded here or anywhere in git — reference the

Alarm.com user by name only.

  1. 7. Mandatory rotation guardrail (Junyan): the moment the code is sent, the system files a row

in ✅ Actions to Perform that the emergency code must be reset — naming recipient, time, studio,

and booking. Plus a loud notice to Junyan and Ela and a J28 ledger entry: a master key leaving the

building is never quiet. Residual risk accepted and banked: if rotation is not actually done,

the guardrail decays into exactly the ignored-queue-row disease this ticket exists to cure —

the reset row must be treated as urgent-until-confirmed, and its rot is the first thing to audit.

  1. 8. All four rungs build together (Junyan, 2026-08-03) — not 1–3 first. Sequenced 1→4 inside one

build ticket so the ladder cannot ship permanently three-quarters done.

Acceptance test (unchanged — replay Jul 24)

Flag raised 06:02, booking 19:30. Under this design: Ela is told at ~06:02; the gate re-verifies

every 30 min all day; Junyan is paged at 17:30 with two hours of runway; at 18:45, if still

broken, Vicky receives the emergency code and a reset row is filed. The 13.5-hour silence is gone.

What this ticket did NOT settle

The other bookend check families in the Question below — existence invariants, reconciliation

invariants, completeness sweeps — were not designed here. They need the cloud checker to exist

first. Graduated as 044, blocked by 043.

Motivating failure — the Vicky Yeung lockout, 2026-07-24 (READ THIS FIRST)

A renter was actually locked out of 509A at 19:30 on Jul 24. This is the concrete cost of

the missing verification layer, and the acceptance test for whatever this ticket builds.

The timeline:

  1. 1. Jul 23, 06:02 — the Doorman's live check returned manual_no_match for Vicky Yeung (no

Alarm.com user at all). It suppressed the alert, reasoning that a prior Access/PIN row

("Extend Vicky's window to cover Jul 24 + Jul 26") was already marked Processed. A full

day of lead time was lost to a Notion row status that did not reflect the panel.

  1. 2. Jul 24, 06:02 — the Doorman got the same manual_no_match, and this time correctly raised

an Action row at Pending Review, explicitly noting the earlier fix had not landed on the panel.

  1. 3. 06:02 → 19:30 (13.5 hours) — the row sat in the queue. Nothing re-checked it, nothing

escalated as the booking approached, nobody actioned it.

  1. 4. 19:30 — Vicky arrived and could not get in. Confirmed by Junyan.
  2. 5. 19:36 — an Alarm.com user was created by hand (window 19:36 Jul 24 → Aug 24). She got in

~6 min late. Jul 26 repeat booking now verifies pass.

Two distinct defects, only one of which is fixed:

  • - (A) FIXED 2026-07-24 in the Doorman prompt — a PRIME DIRECTIVE ("the live panel is the only

truth; a Processed/Done row NEVER suppresses an alert; dedup only against open rows"),

urgent TODAY <HH:MM> — titling for same-day gaps, Raised by = The Doorman (was the retired

Opener), and a second independent access look at 04:00 (step A5) so a broken gate surfaces

~2h earlier with the whole day to fix it.

  • - (B) STILL OPEN — this ticket. *An urgent, correctly-raised flag with a hard deadline sat in a

queue for 13.5 hours and nothing noticed.* Detection was never the problem on Jul 24 — the desk

knew at 06:02. What is missing is the closing half: nothing verifies that a flagged gate

got fixed before the renter arrives. More detection cannot fix this; only an escalating,

deadline-aware check can.

The invariant this ticket must produce (acceptance test — replay Jul 24 against it):

No booking may start while an Access / PIN flag against it is still open. Re-verify against the
live panel (not the row status) on a schedule as the booking approaches; escalate louder as
the clock runs down; and if it is still unresolved close to the start time, put it somewhere a
human is guaranteed to see that hour — not in a queue that is read once a morning.

Open questions for Junyan: which surface escalates (Concierge 2-hourly pass / desk_invariants_v2 /

the Day Sheet's Actions tab), at what thresholds, and who gets pinged when it is 2h out and still

broken — Ops Lead (Ela) by default, but Brazil means it cannot rely on Junyan noticing.

Rehoming: DONE 2026-07-25 (the orphaned-jobs half of this ticket)

Junyan's diagnosis, confirmed: this was an incomplete retirement handoff, not a bug. Ticket 007

retired the Opener and Closer without giving their jobs an active owner, and the Custodian was only

told to detect problems after they appear. Every orphan is now re-homed to a robot that already

reads the same data — no new scheduled agents:

| Orphaned job | New owner | Where |

|---|---|---|

| Unpaid-charge scan (Opener Step 8) | Concierge JOB 32, HEAVY-AM | library/concierge-orchestration.md (git canon) |

| End-of-day booking review + PBF reconciliation + incident sweep (Closer E2/E3) | Concierge JOB 33, HEAVY-PM | same file |

| Yesterday's working-hours reconcile (Opener 7B) | The Timekeeper | trigger prompt |

| Next-day staff shift messages (Closer E4) | The Planner, Step T5 | trigger prompt |

Two cross-cutting rules went in with them:

  • - Every re-homed job must report its own result each pass — including zero — and file a

Type = Run Error row if it cannot run. *A job whose failure looks identical to a quiet success

is exactly how this went unnoticed for two days.*

  • - JOB 32 never derives a price (Junyan's 2026-07-23 pricing rule supersedes the Opener's old

arithmetic): Amount stays blank, Basis records only observable facts. A derived number becomes

a wrong charge to a real renter.

Raised by fallback fixed too — the Actions DB had no option for the robots actually writing

rows, so their true source only survived in free-text Notes. Added: The Doorman, The Sentinel,

Desk Invariants (needed before desk_invariants_v2's auto-escalation can attribute itself) and

Gap audit. Other is no longer a legitimate destination for a known writer.

Damage found and repaired on the way: 2 unbilled [Unpaid] bookings on Jul 25 (Sara D'Amato

509A 14:00–16:00 ≈$42.94; David Garzon 509A 17:00–18:30 ≈$40.68) had no charge row — both now filed

at Pending Review, Raised by = Gap audit, Amount blank pending a Skedda check. Jul 24 verified

clean. PBF has been unowned since Jul 22 — the backlog needs a one-off catch-up sweep.

Still open — the escalation half (Junyan's rulings, 2026-07-25)

  • - The re-check lives in the checker program (desk_invariants_v2), not an LLM pass — Junyan's

choice. Invariant: no booking may start while an Access / PIN flag against it is open.

Re-verify against the live panel, not the row status, and escalate louder as the clock runs down.

  • - **Escalation reaches BOTH Junyan and Ela, by SMS and Slack. Not a queue entry — a push.
  • - A verifier tool is needed — something that re-runs a gate's check and returns pass/fail on

demand, so "is this actually fixed?" is one call rather than a human opening Alarm.com.

  • - ⚠️ An emergency access code is needed for when neither Junyan nor Ela is reachable. This is the

Brazil-shaped hole: today a lockout at 19:30 depends on a human answering. Design this with

Junyan before building — a standing fallback code is a real security decision (who may receive

it, how it rotates, how its use is logged and reviewed), not a config change.

Blocked on: the desk_invariants_v2 swap is gated on Junyan's explicit go (Phase 0 of the

level-up plan); the SMS/Slack push path exists (message queue +

Slack) but has never carried an operational alert.

Question

Re-scoped 2026-07-23 (Junyan, post-007):** the bookends' real purpose was

verification — the Opener's night-watch item count was it checking the overnight

fleet's work, not doing new work. Retiring the runs (007, Ruling 2) was right, but the

verification layer they carried needs a named, durable home. Per the

level-up plan doctrine, the bottleneck is trusted

verification — so the home should be deterministic invariants + monitor-by-exception

(desk_invariants_v2's structured defects → 3-strike escalation to Actions rows), NOT a

resurrected LLM pass that re-reads everything.

Design with Junyan, check by check from the Opener's old Steps 3/4/6/6B/6C/7/7B/8:

  • - Existence invariants (IPR / Overtime / FBS itinerary / watchlist freshness /

Day Sheet freshness): pure date-filtered queries → v2 invariants. The Sentinel covers

run heartbeats; these cover artifact existence — decide if they're one roster or two.

  • - Reconciliation invariants (FBS board ↔ message queue ↔ calendars; duplicate/orphan

board rows; yesterday's hours vs Staff Scheduling): deterministic diffs → v2, with the

Custodian's 09:35 pass as the human-facing reviewer of whatever v2 flags.

  • - Completeness sweeps ([Unpaid] → Money Request Queue incl. prior-day back-check;

GTG no-show surfacing): mostly date math + plan rates — candidate for a small script on

the deterministic lane, with only the discretionary residue routed to Actions rows.

  • - Escalation contract: every failed check must land somewhere a human already looks

(Actions For = Ops Lead / For = Junyan per the 027 split, or the Fixer Report) —

"check exists" is not done until "failure is seen" is wired.

  • - No fire-repair authority (Ruling 2 stands): invariants detect and escalate; the Ops

lead investigates. Nothing auto-refires generators.

Interim coverage until this lands: Custodian 02:35 + 09:35 passes, Sentinel missing-run

detection, desk_invariants v1 hourly. Watch the Fixer Report for rot — unpaid-charge

misses are the most likely first symptom and raise this ticket's urgency.

Coordinate with Phase 0 of the level-up plan (v2 swap is gated on Junyan's explicit go)

— these checks should land as v2 invariants, not a parallel checker.

✅#036🏠Find new homes for the old robot's jobs before switching it off2026-08-01
  • Switching it off once already broke things — it did more than anyone had written down
  • Listed all 13 of its jobs; 7 are already covered, 6 still need a home
  • BUILT Jul 28: the fast robot now writes one-off replies itself — a staff member reads and sends each one
  • So a reply is ready in seconds instead of hours, but a human always pushes the send button
  • Also built in guards so nobody ever gets a pile of robot texts: at most 2 automatic replies per person per half-day, and rapid-fire texts get ONE combined reply, not three
  • PROVED FOR REAL Jul 29: Junyan texted "it's too hot in here" and the how-to came back in 90 seconds — no human, no waiting for the hourly robot
  • Same minute, "can I add an hour tonight?" had a written reply waiting for staff — and it was careful: it promised to check, not that the hour was free
  • Two hidden bugs only showed up once real texts came through, and both are fixed — one had been quietly making every fast reply fall back to the slow robot
  • BUILT Jul 31: someone now listens when a renter replies 'still stuck' — an urgent text goes to on-call staff
  • A 'thanks, all good' reply is recognized and closes things out quietly
  • At 901 after dark, a can't-get-in text now calls staff to bring the key — no robot ever mentions key codes
  • Junyan also ruled: only ONE robot writes replies now — the fast one stops composing, ending the two-robots-one-job mixup
  • LIVE Aug 1: all of it is deployed and running (the deploy error was just the wrong Google account signed in)
  • PROVED FOR REAL Aug 1: texted 'what's my code' — got the code back; replied 'still not working' — an urgent alert reached staff in about a minute
  • That's the exact hole that forced us to switch the old robot back on in July
  • The 'thanks, all good' reply stayed quiet too — no false alarm
  • RETIRED Aug 1: the old robot is switched OFF for good. The front desk is two robots now, not three
  • It had sent nothing at all for ten days — the new setup had already taken over the work
  • Big lesson written down: never switch a robot off based on what its instructions SAY it does — check what it actually did
Technical notes

Rehome the Receptionist's jobs, then retire it

Question

Ruled by Junyan 2026-07-25: "We can retire it but we need to find a new place for its

jobs." The 2026-07-24 retirement was reversed the next day because the event-gate

quick-answer lane covered its ⚡ cards but not its other work. This ticket inventories

every job on The Receptionist (lean)

against a home, builds the missing homes, and only then retires the trigger.

Method note (the lesson that created this ticket): the inventory below is taken from

the procedure page AND cross-checked against what the persona actually emitted

(Message Queue rows, Correspondence Log, run reports). The Jul-24 retirement failed

because it was judged against the persona's written scope — whose prompt says

"you never compose reply wording" — while the queue shows it composing

Booking cancellation → move offer (Jul 22). Never retire a persona against its

documented scope alone.

The inventory

| # | Receptionist job (page step) | Covered by | Status |

|---|---|---|---|

| 1 | Inbound sweep, every 15–60 min (Step 2) | event-gate 1-min Phone.com poll | ✅ better |

| 2 | Watchlist gate — who is in the space (Step 3) | event-gate lookup_watchlist | ✅ same source |

| 3 | Card classification, 11 rows (Step 4) | cards.py, 14 cards + wont-lock | ✅ superset |

| 4 | Card auto-send, Mode 2 (Step 5) | queue row → reviewer → send engine | ✅ |

| 5 | Life-safety first-contact summon (Step 6 Case A) | event-gate classifier + actuator | ✅ |

| 6 | Reply-triggered escalation (Step 6 Case B) | — | ❌ GAP 1 |

| 7 | Card B — 901 night lockbox, staff-queued, never auto-send (Step 5.2) | SOP ruling: staff hand off the key in person; no message ever carries the code | ✅ GAP 2 dissolved 2026-07-31 — folded into GAP 1's summon path |

| 8 | Thermostat card → ✅ Actions row for 901 / "can you set it" (Step 4 row 3) | cards.file_actions_row() | ✅ built 2026-07-31 (b79372c) — needs redeploy |

| 9 | Gmail thread hygiene — archive comfort / INBOX-label emergency (Step 5.5) | Concierge JOB 2 triage | ⚠️ GAP 4 — verify |

| 10 | Composed / courtesy replies (undocumented; observed) | Responder (sibling, unbuilt) | ❌ GAP 5 |

| 11 | Positive close-out handling — "thanks/all good" (Step 6 Case B.1) | — | ⚠️ GAP 6 — low harm |

| 12 | Correspondence Log row + dedup (Step 5.4) | J28 SMS poller + two-way Notion sync | ✅ |

| 13 | Heartbeat / rolling run report (Step 7) | n/a once retired | ✅ |

The gaps, and the proposed home for each

GAP 1 — reply-triggered escalation. The most important, and a LIVE promise breach.

Every card ends "if you need assistance, just reply here and we'll jump in." In the

Receptionist that footer is load-bearing: a still-stuck reply to an EMERGENCY-class card

(901 can't-get-in daytime · 901 can't-get-out · leak · code failing at the door) pages

on-call; a comfort-class one goes to the Concierge. The event-gate lane sends the

same footer and nothing listens. Same defect class as the maintenance-ticket promise:

a card that promises a response must be backed by one.

→ Home: event-gate. It already has the actuator (summons SMS + Staff Call Queue

row) and per-sender state. Needs: remember which card class went to which number today,

classify the next inbound as close-out vs still-stuck, and summon on still-stuck +

emergency-class. Deterministic; no LLM needed.

GAP 2 — Card B, 901 night lockbox. → RESCOPED 2026-07-31 (Junyan), and it got smaller.

Original plan: a hold_for_human card flag that queues the row at Pending Review and

skips the reviewer, so a human releases the 5789 code. That card should not exist.

The lockbox ruling is now explicit in the live SOP

(🔐 Artist is having issues with Door Lock,

Case A + Rules): the lockbox is a staff tool, not a message — a staff member opens the

door with the 527 key and hands it over in person, and no agent may ever draft, queue,

or send the code, to anyone, including the test line. A held draft containing 5789 is still

a message containing 5789; hold_for_human would only have made the wrong thing slower.

→ Correct behaviour: the gate says nothing about keys. A broken-lock report is an

EMERGENCY-class inbound → summon staff (the actuator GAP 1 needs anyway). If a renter asks

for the lockbox code directly, the reply is that a staff member will bring the key.

→ Home: the existing emergency lane + GAP 1's escalation. No new card, no new flag.

This gap closes when GAP 1's summon path exists — it has no independent build left.

GAP 3 — thermostat → Actions row. ✅ BUILT 2026-07-31 (commit b79372c). 901 has no

renter control, and its thermostat how-to says *"I've flagged this to staff so they can

adjust it for you"* — nothing was listening. cards.file_actions_row() now raises the

✅ Actions row (Type=Other, For=Ops Lead, Status=Pending Review, Artist relation set,

Basis quoting the inbound) via the same files_* side-effect pattern as

files_maintenance: at MATCH time, so the promise holds even when the reply is held,

deduped, or the card is unpublished. Gated by files_actions_studios: {"901"} — renters

at the other four studios adjust it themselves and get no row. Dedups against an open

row for that artist inside the 12 h window, and fails closed on an unreadable query

(the inverse of the reply-path dedup: a duplicate "go adjust the thermostat" row wastes

staff attention, and the renter already has their reply either way). Every exit path now

reports its side-effect outcomes via _stamp(), so a suppressed reply still logs whether

the row was raised. 145 tests pass (was 142). Needs a gate redeploy to take effect.

GAP 4 — Gmail thread hygiene. ✅ CLOSED 2026-07-31 (Junyan): "This is covered by the

Concierge not the Receptionist." Owner ruling, no build. JOB 2 keeps sole ownership of

inbox state — the Responder is explicitly barred from labelling, archiving, or marking

read (two writers there would corrupt JOB 2's audit invariants). Nothing to verify and

nothing to rehome: this job was never leaving the Concierge.

GAP 5 — composed / courtesy replies. The Julia cancellation→move offer class.

→ RULED by Junyan 2026-07-28: build it in the gate ("Okay let's build it then"),

after his two-lane latency requirement — emergency replies immediate (already at the

1-min floor), courtesy/ticket drafts "generated as fast as possible… for it to be

usable by staff". 2-hourly Concierge drafting was ruled NOT good enough.

→ BUILT 2026-07-28: drafter.py in event-gate. Two lanes on the gate's own

1-minute clock via direct Messages API calls (claude-opus-5, metered — ~$10/mo at

current volume, deliberately off the subscription):

  1. 1. Inline card review — a freshly queued QA- row is reviewed in ~3 s and flipped

to Ready to Send; card replies land in ~90 s instead of ~11 min. The hourly

GitHub Actions sweep stays on as the safety net for anything held.

  1. 2. Composed drafts — no-card verified messages get a DR- row drafted in Junyan's

voice at Pending Review, never auto-released. Staff open the queue and the

reply is already written (~10 s after the poll sees the text).

→ Barrage guards (Junyan's explicit design constraint — "we don't want a

situation where the person gets a barrage of messages"): per-topic 12 h dedup (was

already there) + per-sender cap of 2 auto-releases/12 h (past it, rows hold for a

human) + fact-preservation check in code (a reviewed body may not introduce any

digit-run or URL the canned original lacked) + one-open-draft-per-renter (a burst of

texts updates the single DR- row — process-local map, durable Notion query as the

cold-start backstop) + pure acks ("ok", "thanks 🙏") never draft.

This satisfies the sibling map's Responder scope for the SMS channel; the sibling

tickets should be annotated rather than double-built.

→ VERIFIED LIVE 2026-07-29 (rev event-gate-00010-slc), both lanes, real SMS:

  • - Card lane — "it's too hot in here" 17:29:13 UTC → qa_inline_review: "released",

thermostat card on the phone inside ~90 s. No sweep involved.

  • - Compose lane — "can I add an hour tonight?" 17:30:14 UTC → dr_drafted: true,

row DR-3887-0729-1330 at Pending Review ~70 s after send, correctly hedged

("let me check the calendar… and get back to you shortly") — no price, no

availability promise, not auto-sent.

  • - Guards observed in live traffic: the 15:18 repeat thermostat text was suppressed

(qa_deduped: true), and the compose burst produced exactly one DR- row.

→ Two build defects found and fixed by this live traffic (both were silent):

  1. 1. review_and_release PATCHed a nonexistent Drafted Body property; Notion voids a

PATCH containing any unknown property, so every inline release failed as

held-notion-error and fell back to the hourly sweep (commit 83a9fd6).

  1. 2. A failed compose returned a bare {"drafted": False} with no reason. Now every

failure names itself (dr_why, held-api-<reason>) — the diagnostic gap that made

the 2026-07-29 04:53 UTC failure unreadable.

→ Residual FIXED 2026-07-29 (commit 52c62d9): the 12 h per-topic dedup was

process-local and reset on deploy (visible at 17:28→17:29 — the pre-deploy revision

suppressed the text, the fresh one sent it). cards.recent_card_row_exists() now asks

the queue DB the same question the in-memory map answers, mirroring the draft lane's

_find_open_draft_row. Fails open on a Notion error — a missed dedup costs one

extra helpful reply; a false suppression costs a renter their answer. Needs a gate

redeploy to take effect.

→ Sweep cadence dropped to hourly (same commit): inline review is now the reviewer

of record, so the GitHub Actions sweep only picks up rows inline review held — rare,

and an hour is fine. The native backstop cron is retimed in qa-review.yml; the

cron-job.org job "qa-review 10-min tick" still needs the same change in its web UI

(that job is what actually fires — the native schedule is only a backstop).

GAP 6 — positive close-out. ✅ CLOSED 2026-07-31 (Junyan): "This also should be owned by

the Responder." "Thanks, all good" is a courtesy/acknowledgement reply carrying no

question or request — already the first line of the Responder's Scope. No build; this

records the owner so it stops reading as unassigned. It does NOT fall to the Concierge

anymore (that path was carved out 2026-07-29).

Retirement precondition (replaces the Jul-24 one)

Retire the trigger when GAPs 1–3 are built and verified, GAP 4 is confirmed, and GAP 5

has a ruled home. Not when the card lane ships.

Notes

  • - No harm was done during the Jul 24 12:22 → Jul 26 00:11 retirement window: the only

cards the lane sent in that period went to Junyan's test line, so no real renter hit

the missing escalation ladder.

  • - Running both agents meanwhile is safe — the Receptionist's dedup skips any message

with a Message Queue row for that artist+card newer than the inbound, which is what

the gate writes within ~1 min.

Resolution records: the built escalation/hold/actions-row code, the GAP 4 verification,

Junyan's GAP 5 ruling, and the trigger retirement + page stub.

Update 2026-07-31 (second) — Junyan's gap-by-gap ruling

⚠️ New finding — The Responder and drafter.py overlap on SMS

Both lanes now compose a held draft for a non-ticket SMS inbound from a verified sender.

drafter.py writes DR- rows ~70 s after the poll; The Responder writes its own rows on

a :20/:50 pass with a 3 h lookback. The Responder's dedup check (a) reads the live Message

Queue, so it should skip a DR- row that already exists — but that is dedup by luck of

timing, not by an owned boundary, and two agents doing one job is precisely the

attribution break the probe exists to measure. Evidence it is live, not theoretical:

DR-3887-0729-2044 (event-gate) and The Responder's 21:51 pass both processed the same

2026-07-29 test-line inbound and reached opposite conclusions about whether to draft.

→ RESOLVED 2026-07-31 by owner ruling, recorded in library/the-responder.md: The

Responder is the SOLE composer across ALL channels; the event-gate's composed-draft

lane (drafter.py DR- rows, SMS) is RETIRED. The gate now only sends pre-approved

cards and summons. This picks the opposite branch from the analysis below — the split is

by owner, not by channel+latency — and it is the cleaner rule: one composer, no

attribution ambiguity, nothing to dedup by luck of timing. Known cost, accepted: SMS

composed drafts go from ~70 s to the Responder's ≤30 min cadence. Worth re-visiting only

if a renter-facing complaint traces to that delay. Note the inline CARD review path in

drafter.py is NOT retired — that is the reviewer of record for QA- rows (~3 s) and has

nothing to do with composing.

<details><summary>Superseded analysis (kept for the reasoning, not the conclusion)</summary>

Do not resolve by deleting one lane. They differ in a way that matters: drafter.py

is a ~70 s deterministic-trigger lane on metered Opus (SMS only), The Responder is a

30-min pass with full ledger/calendar/HTR context across SMS and email. The likely

right split is channel + latency (gate owns fast SMS composes; Responder owns email and

anything needing deep context), but that is Junyan's ruling to make, not a build decision.

Owner: this ticket, before retirement — a decommission that leaves two composers running

just moves the ambiguity.

</details>

Same limit throttled the diagnostic itself.

  • - Stagger the runtime windows so :10 / :12 / manual don't hammer the SQL router in the same minute?
  • - Cut SQL reads per pass (fewer dedup queries, cache within a pass)?

  • - Lean on the J28 correspondence ledger (ticket 006) to move correspondence ground-truth off Notion SQL entirely — the dedup queries that 429 are exactly what J28's deterministic store would replace. Coordinate with data-sovereignty-wayfinder and notion-mcp-read-write-quirks.

✅#029📤Send texts with our own program2026-07-21
  • Our own sender replaced the paid sending service
Technical notes

Build the message-queue send engine — retire the Zapier send Zaps

Question

Execute message-queue-send-engine-spec.md

(2026-07-18, Ready to build): replace the Notion SMS/Email sender Zaps with a stateless

Google Apps Script send engine polling the 📤 Zapier Message Queue, dispatching via

Phone.com (SMS) and Gmail (Email), writing Status + Sent At back.

Why this map: 024 closed with "Zapier retains sending only" — this retires that last

send role. It's also another job moving to the deterministic no-persona lane (Brazil-proof:

cloud-hosted, no Mac, no LLM), and it fixes the Zapier snapshot bug that makes scheduled

sends uncancellable (the queue-fbs skill and staff depend on Will Not Send actually

working).

Non-negotiables (from the spec — do not renegotiate here):

  • - No state between polls: cancellation/edits take effect up to the poll before dispatch.
  • - Claim-before-send write ordering + LockService lock (no double-sends).
  • - Giggster rows skipped silently; failures flip to Error + row comment + alert email;

no auto-retry.

  • - In-thread Gmail replies via Source Email thread URL; broken thread → Error, never a

silent new email.

Build steps (AFK where possible):

  1. 1. Schema prep on the queue DB: add Send After (date+time, America/Toronto) and Status

option Error (spec §3.2). Create the Notion internal integration, share with this DB only.

  1. 2. Write the Apps Script (poll → validate §5.1 → dispatch §5.2/§5.3 → write-back §4/§7),

Script Properties per §8. Confirm the current Phone.com v4 SMS endpoint/payload.

  1. 3. Test against a duplicate test copy of the queue DB with test contacts — run all ten

acceptance tests §9, especially ★5 (cancel a scheduled send) and ★6 (late body edit).

Junyan-hands checklist (HITL):

  • - Apps Script project under the Dance Annex Google account; authorize Gmail scope.
  • - Generate Phone.com API credentials in the control panel; paste secrets into Script

Properties (never chat).

  • - Pause the Zapier send Zaps before pointing the engine at the live DB — production

flip, his confirm; never run both.

  • - Week of parallel-watch, then SOP v4 update (📨 Skill: Send Message, page

3867503281c4812fa58ce17353c34aab) and Zapier plan inventory/downgrade.

Resolution records: acceptance-test results (all ten), the Zap-off confirmation, and where

the engine's source of truth lives (script project + any repo mirror).

Progress (2026-07-20 late night) — #3 exposed a real reply-targeting bug; cutover plan v2

  • - #3 first run = mechanically clean but FAILED the intent, and found a production bug.

Ran against the outbound-only "Your Dance Annex Shifts — Tuesday, July 14" thread

(studio-side hex id from the J28 ledger): row claimed Sent, no error — but the reply

never reached Junyan. Cause: GmailThread.reply() replies to the last message's

sender; on any thread where the studio sent last (very common in production), the

reply boomerangs to the studio itself. Fixed in Code.gs v5: reply targets the

latest inbound message (Session.getEffectiveUser() used as "me"); outbound-only

thread → Error, consistent with never-misdirect. Junyan's pasted browser URL was the

new-style #search/…/FMfcgz… token — unusable by getThreadById; production pointers

come from the desk/ledger as hex ids (#all/<hex>, sometimes #inbox/<hex> — parser

already accepts both), so this is a non-issue for real rows.

  • - Cutover plan v2 (Junyan's ruling): keep the Zapier queue machinery alive ≥2 weeks

as backup + second send path. Since the ghost automation triggers on the "In progress"

status group (= Ready to Send), coexistence requires a trigger split: new status

Send via Zapier, ghost automation + any Ready-to-Send Zaps retargeted to it (not

deleted). Full sequence + double-send boundary rule in SETUP.md "Cutover plan v2".

  • - Ledger poller side-check: healthy (local clone was stale; Action runs green, cadence

~hourly rather than the nominal 30 min — no action needed for this ticket).

Schema extended (via update-data-source):

✅#023❓Replace the paid email-logging service?2026-07-17
  • Question: can our own program save email instead?
  • Answer: yes
Technical notes

Replace the Zapier email-logging Zaps with a deterministic Gmail poller?

Question

Junyan's direction (2026-07-16): Zapier email logging is too expensive and error-prone

(git write-races). Decide whether one deterministic poller (the sms_pull.py pattern,

extended to Gmail) replaces the Gmail-in/Gmail-out logging Zaps as the ledger's writer —

and settle auth, runtime, scope, and whether this moots ticket 015's "move off git" premise.

Frame: HANDOFF-email-ledger-poller-decision.md.

Resolution (2026-07-17)

DECIDED: yes — build the Gmail poller and retire both logging Zaps after a one-day

parallel diff. The six handoff questions, ruled:

  1. 1. Auth: OAuth refresh token via a Google Cloud project, not IMAP app-password.

Rationale: the Gmail API gives stable message ids (gmail-<internal id> — the msg_id

scheme already chosen for 006), reads archived + Sent mail with full headers (fixes

the old "Sent readable but no MIME headers" Zap-path limit — re-verified as an API-path

capability, sent-message-groundtruth-capabilities applies to the Zap path only),

and query/history reads make missed runs self-heal like the SMS poller. App passwords

are simpler but fragile (2FA-dependent, silently revocable) — the wrong trade for an

unattended Brazil-window writer. Secret lives as a GitHub Actions secret (refresh

token + client id/secret), same pattern as the watchlist secrets. Mitigate the one

silent-death risk (revoked/expired refresh token) with the watchlist's loud

401-handling pattern: fail the step with a clear message, never skip-green.

  1. 2. Runtime: GitHub Action, piggybacked on the existing 30-min watchlist workflow in

desk-correspondence — one checkout, one runtime, near-zero marginal Actions minutes,

so the combined budget stays where it is today (~900/mo, comfortably under 2,000).

30-min freshness is acceptable: the ledger is a record, not an alerting path (J03/J29

own urgency). Local-Mac runtime rejected — sleep/skip is the exact failure the Brazil

window can't absorb.

  1. 3. Scope: retire Zap 1 (Gmail-in) and Zap 2 (Gmail-out) — nothing else. Per the 006

specs both are logging-only (trigger → GitHub Create/Update File; no labels, no

downstream). Zaps 3/4 are already dead; Zapier remains for sending (SMS send, message

queue) untouched. Side benefits of retirement: the junk-sender filter, the

no-reply@phone.com double-logging bug, and the Get-File-SHA idempotency workaround all

become moot — the poller excludes junk senders in code and is idempotent by filename.

  1. 4. Completeness: the API poller reads by query/history, so agent-archived mail is

captured (the known in:inbox 2-min race disappears) — a reliability gain beyond cost.

  1. 5. Ticket 015 premise: downgraded. The write-race was concurrency, not git. Post-cutover

the repo has two writers — the correspondence poller and the watchlist canon push — but

the watchlist push is already retry-rebase race-tolerant, and the poller adopts the same

retry-rebase push. Git stays the transport; "move the ledger off git" is now optional

performance work, not an urgent race fix. 015's real remaining content is the consumer

repoint + backfill decisions — its body updated accordingly.

  1. 6. One poller or two: unified ingest, two thin pull scripts (sms_pull.py +

gmail_pull.py) invoked by the one Action job. Same effect as a single

correspondence_pull.py (one cost line, one failure surface) without rewriting the

working, smoke-tested SMS poller. Merge later only if the scripts converge naturally.

Migration path (per the handoff, confirmed): build gmail_pull.py → test on a known

thread both directions → run poller + Zaps in parallel one day → diff ledgers → Junyan

turns the two Zaps off (production flip — his confirm required, junyan-working-style).

Graduated: ticket 024 (build gmail_pull.py + Action piggyback + parallel-diff cutover).

Google Cloud project + OAuth consent + secrets are Junyan-hands steps inside 024's checklist.

  1. 1. "Fix it now" — a live wrong state only Junyan can correct (a mis-routed ticket, a wrong held

Actions/For = Junyan. A given mistake files (1) always, and (2) as well when it's a recurring class.

Technical notes

Walk a full Concierge day with Junyan — the lived process, end to end

Question

Junyan's direction (2026-07-16): the 017 evidence pass isn't deep enough — walk through the

WHOLE Concierge process with him across a day's work, so the rearchitecture (018) is designed

Deliverable: a day-in-the-life process record (markdown asset) capturing, hour by hour /

  • - what the desk SHOULD do at each point vs what it currently does (tie back to the

  • - where Junyan's judgment is genuinely needed vs where it's mechanical;
  • - the corrections he makes in real time — the live version of the Sweep Feedback stream;
  • - what "adjusted" looks like to him at each step (his words, not inferred).

Resolution — walked live with Junyan 2026-07-16

The relay: night watch (agent preps tomorrow, report → Ops lead) → Ops lead morning

(verify reports → fix agent errors → release messages → clear gates; manual acts →

desk-drops) → agent sweep → afternoon bulk pass (the real volume) → one evening run →

= the correction stream.

Design rulings produced (the 018 brief):

  1. 1. Unit of work = person/engagement thread, not ticket-per-SOP; SOPs become in-thread

  1. 2. AI-native store; humans get projections — Day Sheet reduces to the human execute-list

+ customer status report. Racetrack model (positions/gates) survives; the Notion-body

substrate does not.

  1. 3. "Messages are just gates" — one gate model for holds, sends, staff actions.
  2. 4. Persistent per-customer context (past threads + correspondence + about-me profile);

off-system staff exchanges captured; everything queryable.

  1. 5. Per-skill sub-agents — routing ten tools through one agent is where execution drops

things; named: money-sweep sub-agent (sweeps all money sources → packages for the human),

FBS day-assistant agent (chatbot-like, fast, renter-facing, coordinates with the duty

manager via an improved FBS monitor showing queued messages + arrivals/departures +

issues-to-clear → sharpens ticket 020's scope).

Unblocks: 018 (now waits only on 006).

Technical notes

Restore the cloud Concierge (:10) runtime — it is firing zero passes

Question

The reliability findings show the authorized cloud Concierge runtime [Claude] :10 (trig_01XVReGFYaBMcLrDovBMevzp) has logged ZERO passes across 07-13 → 07-15 — the desk is being carried by Codex :12 (07-13 only) and Junyan's manual [Claude-local] kicks (07-14, 07-15).   • - Is the trigger disabled, mis-scheduled (UTC-cron / DST — see dance-annex-persona-renames-and-cloud-shifts), or is its report-write step silently failing so it fires but doesn't log? Resolution — CLOSED 2026-07-15

Root cause (diagnosed via live RemoteTrigger get): the cloud trigger trig_01XVReGFYaBMcLrDovBMevzp is simply enabled: false. It was flipped off on 2026-07-13 ~01:51 UTC (updated_at), ~2 min after its last real fire (last_fired_at: 2026-07-13T01:49:19Z) — consistent with being switched off to avoid double-running while Junyan ran the local runtime by hand. Not a schedule/DST bug and not a silent report-write failure:

  • - cron_expression: "10 0-2,12-23 *" is correct — :10 past the hour, UTC 0-2 + 12-23 = the ~12 Toronto daytime fires (08:10–22:10 Toronto), matching the banner.
  • - It logged passes normally right up until it was disabled; nothing about the report-write path is broken.
  • - Banner check: the 🚦 production banner on The Concierge (updated 2026-07-13) explicitly authorizes three redundant runtimes for the identical workflow — (A) Claude cloud :10 = this trigger, (B) Codex :12, (C) local Mac the-concierge (manual). So the gate authorizes :10; the runtime was merely out of sync with its own banner. Re-enabling would need no banner edit — it's a one-field, fully reversible flip.

Fix: DEFERRED by Junyan's ruling (2026-07-15). Presented the finding + the one-field flip. Junyan ruled keep cloud :10 OFF, rely on local (C) only (chosen over "enable now alongside local" and "enable at a pre-departure cutover"). Fix is therefore fix-ready-but-deferred: whenever enabled:true is wanted, flip it via RemoteTrigger update — no other change required. The ticket did NOT observe an unattended :10 pass (the original success criterion) because the authorized outcome is that :10 does not run.

Codex #desk-drops capability decision (2026-07-15): Junyan chose "Investigate giving Codex Slack read" (over accepting the gap or a manual fallback). Graduated to a new ticket — Can the Codex runtime be granted Slack #desk-drops read?.

Open risk flagged (NOT resolved here): local (C) is manual-kick and its hourly schedule "frequently skips" (Mac sleep + App Nap — local-tasks-miss-runs-on-sleep). Under the local-only ruling, nothing fires unattended during Brazil (dep. Jul 27) once manual kicks stop, and Codex :12 has only ever logged passes on 07-13. This is a genuine unattended-coverage gap left open by the ruling; banked to the map's Not yet specified as the Brazil-window coverage question (Junyan has a plan or will re-decide nearer departure).

Unblocks: Cadence right-sizing (004) — its premise shifts from "right-size a trigger firing zero passes" to "right-size the local runtime's job bundle," but the cadence question stands and is now answerable.

Technical notes

Can the Codex runtime be granted Slack #desk-drops read?

Question

Graduated from Restore the cloud Concierge (:10) runtime (012) — Junyan's ruling 2026-07-15: investigate whether the ChatGPT/Codex runtime (:12) can be given Slack read access to #desk-drops (channel C0BFNUTH22H).

Today Codex :12 reports #desk-drops as "unavailable to Codex per live CAPABILITIES," so every Codex pass skips JOB 4 (the Slack drops scan). With cloud :10 held off (012 ruling) and local (C) manual-kick only, this leaves the drops scan single-runtime-dependent — acute over the Brazil window (dep. Jul 27) when only unattended runtimes fire.

Investigate and record:

  • - Is Slack read grantable to the Codex runtime at all? Check the ChatGPT desktop Scheduled Task / connector model for a Slack MCP or equivalent — is the block a missing connector (fixable) or a structural runtime limitation (not fixable from our side)?
  • - If grantable: what's the exact provisioning step, and does it cover C0BFNUTH22H specifically?
  • - If not: what is the fallback so drops aren't lost when :10 is off — a manual/scheduled sweep, or routing drops to a channel/store Codex can read (coordinate with the J28 correspondence ledger, ticket 006)?

Resolution records the feasibility verdict, the provisioning step (or the confirmed structural block), and the chosen fallback if not grantable. AFK where possible; hand Junyan a checklist if provisioning needs his ChatGPT/Slack admin access.

Resolution (2026-07-15, corrected same day by Junyan)

NOT reliably grantable via connector. Junyan's direct experience: the ChatGPT Slack connector cannot read #desk-drops properly; the only working path is Codex reading Slack via its in-app browser. Full findings + corrected implications: 013b-codex-slack-findings.md.

  • - The AFK analysis initially read the Wave-1 shadow (07-11) as proof of connector read; Junyan's ruling overrides — the connector does not work in practice. CAPABILITIES' "UNAVAILABLE" Codex row was substantively correct.
  • - Browser read works but is fragile for unattended scheduled fires (session expiry, harness may not get the browser) — usable interim, not a Brazil-window plan.
  • - Chosen fallback / durable fix: the J28 correspondence ledger (ticket 006) — Zapier files drops into the owned store, making the drops scan runtime-independent. Promoted from fallback to primary.
  • - Optional CAPABILITIES row touch-up (Junyan-approved wording in findings): note the browser-read-only path, read + report, no marking — drops stay unprocessed until a Claude pass ✅-reacts.
